\displaystyle\log_{l}(-\log(s))+\log(1+S_{l-1}(s,\tau))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log_{l}(-\log(s))\bigl(1+\frac{\log(1+S_{l-1}(s,\tau))}{\log_{l}(-\log(s))}\bigl)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log_{l}(-\log(s))(1+S_{l}(s,\tau)).

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim}}

Note that by the claim

\displaystyle T_{m}(s,\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(T_{m-1}(s,\tau))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(\log_{m-1}(-\log(s))(1+S_{m-1}(s,\tau)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(\log_{m-1}(-\log(s)))+\log(1+S_{m-1}(s,\tau))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log_{m}(-\log(s))+\log^{*}(1+S_{m-1}(s,\tau)).

By the claim and Remark 1.25(2) we obtain immediately that
T_{m}
is globally subanalytic in
\tau
with log-analytic support function. So Proposition 5.72 is proven.
\blacksquare

5.73 Proposition

Let
k=0
and
\mathcal{Q}_{0}:=\mathcal{P}_{0,k}
. There is a regular
0
-persistent function
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with
M<|{\mu_{r}-\mu_{0}}|
such that for
D:=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{0},M)
and every
l\in\{0,...,r\}
the function

z_{l}^{*}:D(\mu_{0})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto z_{l}(\mu_{0}(t)+\sigma_{0}se^{i\tau}),

is globally subanalytic in
\tau
with log-analytic support function.

Proof

We may assume that
C
is near with respect to
\mu_{0}
. Otherwise we are done with Remark 5.46 by choosing
M=0
. Note that
\sigma_{1}=-1
,
\sigma_{2}=...=\sigma_{r}=1
and that
\Theta_{1}=...=\Theta_{r}=0
by Proposition 5.47. By Definition 5.2 we have
\mu_{0}=\Theta_{0}
,
\mu_{1}=\Theta_{0}+\sigma_{0}
and for
j\in\{2,...,r\}
we have
\mu_{j}=\Theta_{0}+\sigma_{0}e^{-e_{j-1}}
where
e_{0}:=0
and
e_{m}:=\mathrm{exp}(e_{m-1})
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that for
(t,z)\in H

z_{1}(t,z)=\log(\sigma_{0}z_{0}(t,z))

and

z_{l}(t,z)=\log_{l-1}(-\log(\sigma_{0}z_{0}(t,z)))

for
l\in\{2,...,r\}
. Let

c:=\frac{1}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-1}(2\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}})}.

Then
c<1/\mathrm{exp}_{r-1}(1)=1/e_{r}=|{\mu_{r}(t)-\mu_{0}(t)}|
for
t\in\pi(C)
. Consider
M:=c
. With Remark 5.62 we see that

D(\mu_{0})=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{0},c)(\mu_{0})=\pi(C)\textnormal{}\times\textnormal{}]0,c[\textnormal{}\times\textnormal{}]-\pi,\pi[.

Note that

z_{0}^{*}(t,s,\tau)=\sigma_{0}se^{i\tau}=\sigma_{0}s(\cos(\tau)+i\sin(\tau))
and
z_{1}^{*}(t,s,\tau)=\log(s)+i\tau
for
(t,s,\tau)\in D(\mu_{0})
.
So
z_{0}^{*}
and
z_{1}^{*}
fulfill the desired properties since the restriction of the global sine and cosine function on
]-\pi,\pi[
are globally subanalytic. We obtain for
l\in\{1,...,r-1\}

\displaystyle z_{l+1}^{*}(t,s,\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log_{l}(-\log(\sigma_{0}z_{0}(t,\Theta_{0}(t)+\sigma_{0}se^{i\tau})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log_{l}(-\log(se^{i\tau}))

for every
(t,s,\tau)\in D(\mu_{0})
. With Proposition 5.72 we see that

]0,c[\textnormal{}\times\textnormal{}]-\pi,\pi[\textnormal{}\to\mathbb{C},(s,\tau)\mapsto\log_{l}(-\log(se^{i\tau})),

is a well-defined globally subanalytic function in
\tau
with log-analytic support function for
l\in\{0,...,r-1\}
and we are done with the proof of Proposition 5.73.
\blacksquare

5.74 Proposition

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{0}:=\mathcal{P}_{0,k}
. There is a regular
k
-persistent function
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with
M<|{\mu_{r}-\mu_{k}}|
such that for
D:=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{0},M)
abd every
l\in\{0,...,r\}
the function

z_{l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto z_{l}(\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
.

Proof

For
j\in\{0,...,k\}
we set
\mathcal{Q}_{j}:=\mathcal{P}_{j,k}
.
We may assume that
C
is near with respect to
\mu_{k}
. Otherwise we are done with Remark 5.46 by choosing
M=0
. Note that
\sigma_{k+1}=-1
,
\sigma_{k+2}=...=\sigma_{r}=1
and that
\Theta_{k+1}=...=\Theta_{r}=0
by Proposition 5.47. So for
(t,z)\in H
we have with Corollary 5.19

z_{k+1}(t,z)=\log(\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))=\log(\sigma_{k}\mathcal{Q}_{k}(t,z))

and

z_{l}(t,z)=\log_{l-k-1}(-\log(\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z)))=\log_{l-k-1}(-\log(\sigma_{k}\mathcal{Q}_{k}(t,z)))

for
l\in\{k+2,...,r\}
.

If
k=0
we are done with Proposition 5.73. So assume
k>0
.

Fix
\lambda\in\textnormal{}]0,1/2[
such that

\bigl|{\frac{\log(1+z)-z}{z}}\bigl|<1/2

for
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
with
|{z}|<\lambda
. For
l\in\{1,...,k\}
set

\zeta_{l}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{>0},t\mapsto\min\Bigl\{\tfrac{1}{e^{2|{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}|}},\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-k-1}(2\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}})},

\lambda e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)},\tfrac{e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-k-1}(2\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}})}\Bigl\}.

We see that
\zeta_{l}
is regular (by Remark 5.55 and Example 5.56(1)) and
k
-persistent (by Example 5.43 and Remark 5.42(3)) for every
l\in\{1,...,k\}
. For
l\in\{1,...,k\}
set

\Omega_{l}:=\{(t,z)\in H\mid|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\zeta_{l}(t)\}.

By Proposition 5.67(1) there is a regular
k
-persistent
M_{l}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{0},M_{l})\subset\Omega_{l}
for
l\in\{1,...,k\}
. Consider

M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{|{\mu_{r}(t)-\mu_{k}(t)}|,M_{1}(t),...,M_{k}(t)\}.

Then
M
is
k
-persistent and regular. We have
D:=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{0},M)\subset\bigcap_{l=1}^{k}\Omega_{l}.

Clearly
D_{t}
is connected and
D_{t}\cap\mathbb{R}\neq\emptyset
for
t\in\pi(D)
. Let for
l\in\{1,...,k\}

c_{l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\frac{\sigma_{l-1}\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}{e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}}.

Then for every
(t,z)\in D
and
l\in\{1,...,k\}
we have

\mathcal{Q}_{l}(t,z)=\log(1+c_{l}(t,z)).

Let
\mathcal{V}\in\{c,\mathcal{Q}\}
. By Remark 5.21 we have

(\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})\mathcal{V}_{l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{C}^{-},

and

(\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})\mathcal{V}_{l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

if
z\in\mathbb{R}
for every
(t,z)\in D
and
l\in\{1,...,k\}
.
\textnormal{ }(+_{\mathcal{V}})

Note also that

|{\mathcal{V}_{l}(t,z)}|<\frac{1}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-k-1}\bigl(2\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}}\bigl)}

for every
(t,z)\in D
and
l\in\{1,...,k\}
.
(++_{\mathcal{V}})

By shrinking
M
if necessary we may assume with Corollary 5.68(1) that

\mathcal{V}_{l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto\mathcal{V}_{l}(t,\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
for every
l\in\{0,...,k\}
. (Therefore

z_{l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto z_{l}(t,\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is also free-regular in
s
.)
\textnormal{ }(+++_{\mathcal{V}})

For
l\in\{0,...,k\}
and
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
we define

R_{m,l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\log_{m}(-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})\mathcal{Q}_{l}(t,z))).

By Remark 5.71,
(+_{\mathcal{Q}})
and
(++_{\mathcal{Q}})
we obtain that the function
R_{m,l}
is well-defined, continuous in
z
, and for
(t,z)\in D
we have
R_{m,l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{C}^{-}
and if
z\in\mathbb{R}
then
R_{m,l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
(+_{R})

Note that
\sigma_{k+m+1}z_{k+m+1}=R_{m,k}
for
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. So the goal is to show that the corresponding
R_{m,l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{C}
is free-regular in
s
by an induction on the natural numbers
l
and
m
. But the problem is that we have to deal with iterated logarithms. So we have to consider some more auxiliary functions and establish additionally properties for them (compare with Claim 1 to Claim 4).

For
l\in\{1,...,k\}
we set

S_{0,l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\mu_{k-l,k}(t),

and inductively for
m\in\{1,...,r-k-1\}

S_{m,l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\log\Bigl(1+\frac{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z)}\Bigl).

Let for
l\in\{1,...,k\}
and
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}

T_{m,l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\log_{m}(-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})c_{l}(t,z))).

By Remark 5.71,
(+_{c})
and
(++_{c})
we obtain that the function
T_{m,l}
is well-defined, continuous in
z
, and for
(t,z)\in D
we have
T_{m,l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{C}^{-}
and if
z\in\mathbb{R}
then
T_{m,l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
(+_{T})

Let for
l\in\{1,...,k\}

b_{l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\frac{\log(1+c_{l}(t,z))-c_{l}(t,z)}{c_{l}(t,z)},

U_{0,l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto-\log(1+b_{l}(t,z)),

and inductively for
m\in\{1,...,r-k-1\}

U_{m,l}:D\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\log\Bigl(1+\frac{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{T_{m-1,l}(t,z)}\Bigl).

Note that
|{c_{l}(t,z)}|<\lambda
since
|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\lambda e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}
for
(t,z)\in D
. So we see that
b_{l}
is well-defined, continuous in
z
,
|{b_{l}(t,z)}|<1/2
by the choice of
\lambda
and
1+b_{l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{C}^{-}
for
(t,z)\in D
.
(+_{b})

Claim 1

Let
l\in\{1,...,k\}
. The function
S_{m-1,l}
is well-defined, continuous in
z
and for
(t,z)\in D
we have

\Bigl|\frac{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z)}\Bigl|<1/2

for every
m\in\{1,...,r-k-1\}
.

Proof of Claim 1

Note that for
(t,z)\in D

|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\min\bigl\{\tfrac{1}{e^{2|{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}|}},\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-k-1}(2\sqrt{\log(3/2)^{2}+\pi^{2}})}\bigl\}.\textnormal{ }(*)

We show Claim 1 by induction on
m\in\{1,...,r-k-1\}
. Fix
(t,z)\in D
.

m=1
: That
S_{0,l}
is well-defined and continuous in
z
is clear. We obtain with
(*)

|{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}|<-1/2\log(|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|)

and with the definition of the complex logarithm

|{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}|<1/2|{\log((\prod_{j=l-1}^{k}\sigma_{j})\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z))}|.

This gives

\Bigl|\frac{S_{0,l}(t,z)}{R_{0,l-1}(t,z)}\Bigl|<1/2.

m-1\to m:

We obtain well-definability of
S_{m-1,l}
by the inductive hypothesis. We get

\displaystyle|{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl|{\log\bigl(1+\frac{S_{m-2,l}(t,z)}{R_{m-2,l-1}(t,z)}\bigl)}\bigl|

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}}

by the inductive hypothesis and definition of the complex logarithm. So we obtain by
(*)

|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\frac{1}{\mathrm{exp}_{m}(2|{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}|)},

i.e.

2|{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}|<\log_{m-1}(-\log(|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|)).

By the definition of the complex logarithm we obtain that

\Bigl|\frac{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z)}\Bigl|<1/2

and we are done since
R_{m-1,l-1}
is continuous in
z
.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }1}

By Claim 1 and
(+_{R})
we have that
S_{m,l}
is well-defined, continuous in
z
and

1+\frac{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z)}\in\mathbb{C}^{-}

for
(t,z)\in D
,
l\in\{1,...,k\}
and
m\in\{1,...,r-k-1\}
.
(+_{S})

Claim 2

Let
l\in\{1,...,k\}
. The function
U_{m-1,l}
is well-defined, continuous in
z
and for
(t,z)\in D
we have

\Bigl|\frac{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{T_{m-1,l}(t,z)}\Bigl|<1/2

for every
m\in\{1,...,r-k-1\}
.

Proof of Claim 2

Fix
(t,z)\in D
. We have

|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\frac{e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-k-1}(2\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}})}

and by
(+_{b})

|{U_{0,l}(t,z)}|<\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}}.\textnormal{ }(**)

We do an induction on
m
.

m=1
: With
(+_{b})
we obtain that
U_{0,l}
is well-defined and continuous in
z
. With
(**)
we obtain

|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\frac{e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}}{\mathrm{exp}(2\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}})}

and therefore

|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\frac{e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}}{\mathrm{exp}(2|{U_{0,l}(t,z)}|)}.

This gives

|{U_{0,l}(t,z)}|<-1/2\log(|{c_{l}(t,z)}|)

and with the definition of the complex logarithm

|{U_{0,l}(t,z)}|<1/2|{\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})c_{l}(t,z))}|.

We obtain

\Bigl|{\frac{U_{0,l}(t,z)}{T_{0,l}(t,z)}}\Bigl|<1/2.

m-1\to m:

We obtain well-definability of
U_{m-1,l}
by the inductive hypothesis. We get

\displaystyle|{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl|{\log\bigl(1+\frac{U_{m-2,l}(t,z)}{T_{m-2,l}(t,z)}\bigl)}\bigl|

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\sqrt{\log^{2}(3/2)+\pi^{2}}

by the inductive hypothesis. This gives with
(**)

|{\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z)}|<\frac{e^{\mu_{k-l,k}(t)}}{\mathrm{exp}_{m}(2|{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}|)}.

This implies

2|{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}|<\log_{m-1}(-\log(|{c_{l}(t,z)}|))

and with the definition of the complex logarithm we obtain

\Bigl|{\frac{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{T_{m-1,l}(t,z)}}\Bigl|<1/2

and are done. (We obtain the continuity of
U_{m-1,l}
in
z
since
T_{m-2,l}
is continuous in
z
.)

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }2}

By Claim 2 we have that
U_{m,l}
is well-defined, continuous in
z
, and we have

1+\frac{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{T_{m-1,l}(t,z)}\in\mathbb{C}^{-}

for
(t,z)\in D
,
l\in\{1,...,k\}
and
m\in\{1,...,r-k-1\}
.
(+_{U})

Claim 3

Let
(t,z)\in D
. Let
l\in\{1,...,k\}
. It holds

T_{m,l}(t,z)=R_{m,l-1}(t,z)+S_{m,l}(t,z)

for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
.

Proof of Claim 3

We do an induction on
m
.

m=0
: We obtain

\displaystyle T_{0,l}(t,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})c_{l}(t,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\log((\prod_{j=l-1}^{k}\sigma_{j})\mathcal{Q}_{l-1}(t,z))+\mu_{k-l,k}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{0,l-1}(t,z)+S_{0,l}(t,z).

m-1\to m
:
By Proposition 5.70,
(+_{R})
,
(+_{S})
,
(+_{T})
we obtain with the inductive hypothesis

\displaystyle\log(T_{m-1,l}(t,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z)+S_{m-1,l}(t,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z))+\log\Bigl(1+\frac{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z)}\Bigl)

and therefore

T_{m,l}(t,z)=\log(T_{m-1,l}(t,z))=R_{m,l-1}(t,z)+S_{m,l}(t,z).

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }3}

Claim 4

Let
(t,z)\in D
. Let
l\in\{1,...,k\}
. It holds

R_{m,l}(t,z)=T_{m,l}(t,z)+U_{m,l}(t,z)

for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
.

Proof of Claim 4

We do an induction on
m
.

m=0
: We obtain

\displaystyle R_{0,l}(t,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})\mathcal{Q}_{l}(t,z))=-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})\log(1+c_{l}(t,z)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})(c_{l}(t,z)+\log(1+c_{l}(t,z))-c_{l}(t,z)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})c_{l}(t,z)(1+b_{l}(t,z))).

With
(+_{b})
,
(+_{c})
,
(+_{\mathcal{Q}})
and Proposition 5.70 we obtain

-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})c_{l}(t,z)(1+b_{l}(t,z)))=-\log((\prod_{j=l}^{k}\sigma_{j})c_{l}(t,z))-\log(1+b_{l}(t,z)).

Therefore

R_{0,l}=T_{0,l}(t,z)+U_{0,l}(t,z).

m-1\to m
: By Proposition 5.70,
(+_{R})
,
(+_{T})
,
(+_{U})
and the inductive hypothesis we obtain

\displaystyle\log(R_{m-1,l}(t,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(T_{m-1,l}(t,z)+U_{m-1,l}(t,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log(T_{m-1,l}(t,z))+\log\Bigl(1+\frac{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{T_{m-1,l}(t,z)}\Bigl)

and therefore

\displaystyle R_{m,l}(t,z)=\log(R_{m-1,l}(t,z))=T_{m,l}(t,z)+U_{m,l}(t,z).

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }4}

Now we are able to show that the corresponding
R_{m,l}^{*}
is free-regular in
s
: The idea is to show simultaneously that
T_{m,l}^{*}
and
R_{m,l}^{*}
are free-regular in
s
for
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
by doing an induction on
l
and using Claim 1 to Claim 4.

Claim 5

(I_{l})

for
l\in\{1,...,k\}
: The function

T_{m,l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto T_{m,l}(t,\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
.

(II_{l})

for
l\in\{0,...,k\}
: The function

R_{m,l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto R_{m,l}(t,\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
.

Proof of Claim 5

We start with
(II_{0})
. Let
(t,s,\tau)\in D(\mu_{k})
. We have

0<s<\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-k-1}(2\sqrt{\log(3/2)^{2}+\pi^{2}})}

since

|{z-\mu_{k}(t)}|=|{\mathcal{Q}_{0}(t,z)}|<\tfrac{1}{\mathrm{exp}_{r-k-1}(2\sqrt{\log(3/2)^{2}+\pi^{2}})}

for all
(t,z)\in D
. Note that

R_{m,0}^{*}(t,s,\tau)=\log_{m}(-\log(se^{i\tau}))

for
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. Therefore with Proposition 5.72 and Remark 5.65(2) we are done.

Assume that
(I_{l-1})
and
(II_{l-1})
have already been shown for
l\in\{1,...,k\}
where
(I_{0})
is any true statement.
We show
(I_{l})
. By Claim 3 we have
T_{m,l}=R_{m,l-1}+S_{m,l}
on
D
for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. With
(II_{l-1})
and Remark 5.66(2) it suffices to show that

S_{m,l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto S_{m,l}(t,\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. We do an induction on
m
. For
m=0
this is clear since
S_{0,m}^{*}
considered as a function on
\pi(D)
is the restriction of the regular function
\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R},t\mapsto\mu_{k-l,k}(t)
. So with Definition 5.64 one sees immediately that
S_{0,m}^{*}
is free-regular in
s
. Assume
m>0
. By Claim 1 we obtain that

\Bigl|{\frac{S_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{R_{m-1,l-1}(t,z)}}\Bigl|<1/2

for every
(t,z)\in D
. So

S_{m,l}=\log^{*}\Bigl(1+\frac{S_{m-1,l}}{R_{m-1,l-1}}\Bigl).

With
(II_{l-1})
, the inductive hypothesis and Remark 5.66(2) we are done.

We show
(II_{l})
. By Claim 4 we have
R_{m,l}=T_{m,l}+U_{m,l}
on
D
for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. With
(I_{l})
and Remark 5.66(2) it suffices to show that

U_{m,l}^{*}:D(\mu_{k})\to\mathbb{R},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto U_{m,l}(t,\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. We do an induction on
m
.

m=0
: Note that
U_{0,l}(t,z)=\log^{*}(1+b_{l}(t,z))
and

b_{l}(t,z)=\frac{\log^{*}(1+c_{l}(t,z))-c_{l}(t,z)}{c_{l}(t,z)}

for every
(t,z)\in D
(compare with
(+_{b})
and
(++_{c})
). With
(+++_{c})
and Remark 5.66(2) we are done.

m-1\to m
:
By Claim 4 we obtain that

\Bigl|\frac{U_{m-1,l}(t,z)}{T_{m-1,l}(t,z)}\Bigl|<1/2

for every
(t,z)\in D
. So

U_{m,l}=\log^{*}\Bigl(1+\frac{U_{m-1,l}}{T_{m-1,l}}\Bigl).

With
(I_{l})
, the inductive hypothesis and Remark 5.66(2) we are done.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }5}

We have

\sigma_{k+m+1}z_{k+m+1}|_{D}=R_{m,k}

for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. Therefore we obtain with
(II_{k})
in Claim 5 that
z_{k+m+1}^{*}
is free-regular in
s
for every
m\in\{0,...,r-k-1\}
. By
(+++_{\mathcal{Q}})
we also have that
z_{l}^{*}
is free-regular in
s
for
l\in\{0,...,k\}
. This finishes the proof of Proposition 5.74.
\blacksquare

5.75 Corollary

Let
q:=(q_{0},...,q_{r})\in\mathbb{Q}^{r+1}
. There is a
k
-persistent regular function
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with
M<|{\mu_{r}-\mu_{k}}|
such that for
D:=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,k},M)
the function

\mathcal{Z}_{\mu_{k}}^{\otimes q}:D(\mu_{k})\mapsto\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,\mu_{k}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{k}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is bounded in
\tau
and free-regular in
s
.

Proof

The proof for this corollary is the same as the proof of Corollary 5.69.
\blacksquare

5.1.4
Holomorphic Extensions of Prepared Restricted

Log-Exp-Analytic Functions in one Variable and the Desired Result on Integration

In this section we compute a definable holomorphic extension
F:\Lambda\to\mathbb{C}
of a prepared restricted log-exp-analytic function
f:C\to\mathbb{R}
in one variable and construct for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k^{\textnormal{ch}},r\}
if
k^{\textnormal{ch}}\geq 0
a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the following holds: We have
D:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)\subset\Gamma
and
F|_{D}
is complex log-analytically prepared in
z
with regular data, i.e. of the form

F|_{D}=a(t)(\sigma_{0}z_{0})^{q_{0}}\cdot...\cdot(\sigma_{r}z_{r})^{q_{r}}U(t,z)

where
a(t)
is regular,
q_{0},...,q_{r}\in\mathbb{Q}
and
U=V\circ\phi
where
V
is a convergent complex power series and the components of
\phi
are of the form
b_{j}(t)(\sigma_{0}z_{0})^{p_{0}}\cdot...\cdot(\sigma_{r}z_{r})^{p_{r}}
where
b_{j}(t)
is regular and
p_{0},...,p_{r}\in\mathbb{Q}
. This fact shows that
F|_{D}
depends ”only in a globally subanalytically way” from
z_{0}|_{D},...,z_{r}|_{D}
. Finally we obtain the desired result on integration with our considerations in Section 5.1.3.

At first we define complex log-analytically prepared functions.

5.76 Definition

Let
Y\subset H
be definable. Let
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
F:Y\to\mathbb{C}
be a function. We say that
F
is
complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with center
\Theta
if

F(t,z)=a(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,z)U(t,z)

for all
(t,z)\in Y
where
a
is a definable function on
\pi(Y)
which vanishes identically or has no zero,
q\in\mathbb{Q}^{r+1}
, and
U=V\circ\phi
such that the following holds.

(a)

The function
\phi
is given by

\phi:Y\to\overline{D^{s}(0,1)},(t,z)\mapsto

(b_{1}(t)\prod_{l=0}^{r}(\sigma_{l}z_{l}(t,z))^{p_{1l}},...,b_{s}(t)\prod_{l=0}^{r}(\sigma_{l}z_{l}(t,z))^{p_{sl}}),

where
s\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
b_{1},...,b_{s}:\pi(Y)\to\mathbb{R}
are definable which have no zeros and
p_{1l},...,p_{sl}\in\mathbb{Q}
for every
l\in\{0,...,r\}
.

(b)

V
is a power series which converges absolutely on an open neighbourhood of
\overline{D^{s}(0,1)}
.

We call
a

coefficient
and
b_{1},...,b_{s}
are called
base functions
. We call
\mathcal{LA}_{\mathbb{C}}:=(r,\mathcal{Z},a,q,s,V,b,P)
where
\mathcal{Z}:=(z_{0},...,z_{r})
and
b:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
and

P:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}p_{10}&\cdot&\cdot&p_{1r}\\
\cdot&&&\cdot\\
\cdot&&&\cdot\\
p_{s0}&\cdot&\cdot&p_{sr}\\
\end{array}\right)\in M\big(s\times(r+1),\mathbb{Q})

a
complex LA-preparing tuple
for
F
.

The next Remark shows that a log-analytically prepared function
f:C\to\mathbb{R}
in
x
can be extended to a complex log-analytically prepared function in
z
which is holomorphic in
z
, i.e. there is a definable
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{C}
with
C\subset\Gamma
and a complex
r
-log-analytically prepared function
F:\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
in
z
such that
\Gamma_{t}
is open,
F_{t}
is holomorphic for
t\in\pi(C)
and
F|_{C}=f
.

5.77 Remark

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
f:C\to\mathbb{R}
be
r
-log-analytically prepared in
x
. Let
\mathcal{LA}:=(r,\mathcal{Y},a,q,s,v,b,P)
be a preparing tuple for
f
with
b:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
and
P:=(p_{1},...,p_{s})^{t}
. Let
R>1
be such that
v
converges absolutely on an open neighbourhood of
[-R,R]^{s}
. For
l\in\{1,...,s\}
set

\Gamma_{l}:=\{(t,z)\in H\mid|{b_{l}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{l}}(t,z)}|<R\}.

Set

\Gamma:=\bigcap_{l=1}^{s}\Gamma_{l}

and consider

F:\Gamma\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto a(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,z)\hat{V}(b_{1}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{1}}(t,z),...,b_{s}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{s}}(t,z))

where
\hat{V}
is a complex power series in
s
variables which converges absolutely on an open neighbourhood of
\overline{D^{s}(0,R)}
with
\hat{V}|_{[-1,1]^{s}}=v
. Then the function
F
is well-defined, definable and holomorphic in
z
. Additionally we have
C\subset\Gamma
,
F|_{C}=f
and
F
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with preparing tuple
\mathcal{LA}_{\mathbb{C}}:=(r,\mathcal{Z},a,q,s,V,1/R\cdot b,P)
where
V(z_{1},...,z_{s}):=\hat{V}(Rz_{1},....,Rz_{s})
.

Now we compute a definable holomorphic extension of a prepared restricted log-exp-analytic function in one variable. At first we consider a function
f:C\to\mathbb{R}
which is log-exp-analytically
(e,r)
-prepared with respect to a set
E
of positive definable functions on
C
.

For Section 5.1.4 we fix the following. Let
e\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{-1\}
and
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Fix a function
f:C\to\mathbb{R}
which is log-exp-analytically
(e,r)
-prepared in
x
with center
\Theta
with respect to a finite set
E
of positive definable functions such that every
g\in\log(E)
is log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared in
x
with respect to
E
for a
l\in\{-1,...,e-1\}
(compare with Definition 4.9). For
g\in\log(E)\cup\{f\}
we fix a corresponding preparing tuple
(r,\mathcal{Y},a_{g},\mathrm{exp}(d_{0,g}),q_{g},s,v_{g},b_{g},\mathrm{exp}(d_{g}),P_{g})
where
b_{g}:=(b_{1,g},...,b_{s,g})
and
\mathrm{exp}(d_{g}):=(\mathrm{exp}(d_{1,g}),...,\mathrm{exp}(d_{s,g}))
(compare with Definition 4.9). Fix a finite set
\mathcal{E}
of
C
-heirs such that
\Theta
and
a_{g},b_{g}
can be constructed from
\mathcal{E}
for
g\in\log(E)\cap\{f\}
.

Fix
R>1
such that
v_{g}
converges absolutely on
[-R,R]^{s}
and a complex power series
V_{g}
which converges absolutely on
\overline{D^{s}(0,R)}
with
V_{g}|_{[-1,1]^{s}}=v_{g}
for every
g\in\log(E)\cup\{f\}
. By redefining
a_{g}
if necessary we may suppose that
|{V_{g}}|<\pi
(since
V_{g}
is bounded. This implies also
v_{g}<\pi
).

5.78 Definition

For a log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared function
g\in\log(E)\cup\{f\}
we define by induction on
l\in\{-1,...,e\}
a set
\Lambda_{g}\subset\pi(C)\times\mathbb{C}
and a function
\Phi_{g}:\Lambda_{g}\to\mathbb{C}
which is
complex
(l,r)
-prepared in
z
and a
complex preparing tuple
\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{C}}
for
\Phi_{g}
. To this preparation we assign a set
E_{\Phi_{g}}
of
complex definable functions
on
\Lambda_{g}
.

Base case
:
l=-1
: We set
\Lambda_{g}=\pi(C)\times\mathbb{C}
,
\Phi_{g}=0
,
E_{\phi_{g}}=\emptyset
and
\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{C}}=(0)
.

Inductive Step
: Let

(r,\mathcal{Y},a,e^{d_{0}},q,s,v,b,e^{d},P):=(r,\mathcal{Y},a_{g},e^{d_{0,g}},q_{g},s,v_{g},b_{g},e^{d_{g}},P_{g})

where
b_{g}:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
,
e^{d_{g}}:=(e^{d_{1}},...,e^{d_{s}})
,
P_{g}:=(p_{1},...,p_{s})^{t}
and for
d_{i}
the corresponding
\Lambda_{d_{i}}
, the complex log-exp-analytically
(l-1,r)
-prepared
\Phi_{d_{i}}
in
z
and the corresponding set
E_{\phi_{d_{i}}}
have already been defined for
i\in\{0,...,s\}
. Set

\Lambda_{0,g}:=\{(t,z)\in\Lambda_{d_{0}}\cap H\mid|{\textnormal{Im}(\Phi_{d_{0}}(t,z))}|<\pi\}

and for
i\in\{1,...,s\}
set

\Lambda_{i,g}:=\{(t,z)\in\Lambda_{d_{i}}\cap H\mid|{b_{i}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{i}}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(\Phi_{d_{i}}(t,z))}|<R,|{\textnormal{Im}(\Phi_{d_{i}}(t,z))}|<\pi\}.

Set
\Lambda_{g}=\bigcap_{i=0}^{s}\Lambda_{i,g}
and

\Phi_{g}:\Lambda_{g}\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto a(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,z)e^{\Phi_{d_{0}}(t,z)}

V_{g}(b_{1}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{1}}(t,z)e^{\Phi_{d_{1}}(t,z)},...,b_{s}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{s}}(t,z)e^{\Phi_{d_{s}}(t,z)}).

We set

E_{\Phi_{g}}=(E_{\Phi_{d_{0}}}\cup E_{\Phi_{d_{1}}}\cup...\cup E_{\Phi_{d_{s}}}\cup\{e^{\Phi_{d_{0}}},...,e^{\Phi_{d_{s}}}\})|_{\Lambda_{g}}

and

\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{C}}=(r,\mathcal{Z},a,\mathrm{exp}(\Phi_{d_{0}}),q,s,V_{g},b,\mathrm{exp}(\Phi_{d}),P)

for
\Phi_{g}
where
\mathrm{exp}(\Phi_{d}):=(\mathrm{exp}(\Phi_{d_{1}}),...,\mathrm{exp}(\Phi_{d_{s}}))
.

We often write
F
instead for
\Phi_{g}
and
\Lambda
instead for
\Lambda_{g}
if
g=f
.

5.79 Remark

Let
g\in\log(E)\cup\{f\}
be log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared in
x
with respect to
E
for
l\in\{-1,...,e\}
. We have that
\Lambda_{g}
is definable,
(\Lambda_{g})_{t}
is open for
t\in\pi(C)
and
\Phi_{g}
is holomorphic in
z
. Additionally we have
\Phi_{g}|_{C}=g
and
\Phi_{g}
can be constructed from
E_{\phi_{g}}\cup\mathcal{E}
.

Proof

We show that
\Phi_{g}
can be constructed from
E_{\Phi_{g}}\cup\mathcal{E}
. The other properties are clear. We do an induction on
l
. For
l=-1
there is nothing to show.

l-1\to l:
Let

(r,\mathcal{Y},a,e^{d_{0}},q,s,v,b,e^{d},P):=(r,\mathcal{Y},a_{g},e^{d_{0,g}},q_{g},s,v_{g},b_{g},e^{d_{g}},P_{g})

where
b_{g}:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
,
\mathrm{exp}(d_{g}):=(\mathrm{exp}(d_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(d_{s}))
and
P_{g}:=(p_{1},...,p_{s})^{t}
. Note that
a
and
b
can be constructed from
\mathcal{E}
. Let

\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{C}}:=(r,\mathcal{Z},a,\mathrm{exp}(D_{0}),q,s,V,b,\mathrm{exp}(D),P)

be a complex preparing tuple for
\Phi_{g}
where
D_{j}:=\Phi_{d_{j}}
for
j\in\{0,...,s\}
and

\mathrm{exp}(D):=(\mathrm{exp}(D_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(D_{s})).

Note that
z_{0},...,z_{r}
can be constructed from
\mathcal{E}
by Remark 5.12(3) since
\Theta_{0},...,\Theta_{r}
can be constructed from
\mathcal{E}
. By the inductive hypothesis we have that the functions
D_{0},...,D_{s}
can be constructed from
E_{D_{0}}\cup...\cup E_{D_{s}}\cup\mathcal{E}
. So we obtain that
D_{0}|_{\Lambda_{g}},...,D_{s}|_{\Lambda_{g}}
can be constructed from
(E_{D_{0}}\cup...\cup E_{D_{s}})|_{\Lambda_{g}}\cup\mathcal{E}
and we see with Remark 3.10(1) that the functions
e^{D_{0}}|_{\Lambda_{g}},...,e^{D_{s}}|_{\Lambda_{g}}
can be constructed from
E_{\phi_{g}}\cup\mathcal{E}
since the imaginary part of
D_{j}
is bounded and
e^{D_{j}}|_{\Lambda_{g}}\in E_{\Phi_{g}}
for
j\in\{0,...,s\}
. Let

\eta:\Lambda_{g}\to\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{s}\times\mathbb{C}^{s+1},(t,z)\mapsto(a(t),b_{1}(t),...,b_{s}(t),e^{D_{0}(t,z)},...,e^{D_{s}(t,z)}).

Then
\eta
can be constructed from
E_{\Phi_{g}}\cup\mathcal{E}
.

Claim

There is a globally subanalytic
J:\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{s}\times\mathbb{C}^{s+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r+1}\to\mathbb{C}
such that

\Phi_{g}(t,z)=J(\eta(t,z),z_{0}(t,z),...,z_{r}(t,z))

for every
(t,z)\in\Lambda_{g}
.

Proof of the claim

Let
u:=(u_{0},...,u_{s})
and
y:=(y_{0},...,y_{s})
range over
\mathbb{C}^{s+1}
. Let
q=:(p_{00},...,p_{0r})
. Let
w:=(w_{0},...,w_{r})
range over
\mathbb{C}^{r+1}
. For
l\in\{0,...,s\}
let

\alpha_{l}:\mathbb{C}^{s+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{s+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r+1}\to\mathbb{C},(u,y,w)\mapsto

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{l}y_{l}\prod_{j=0}^{r}(\sigma_{j}w_{j})^{p_{lj}},&\sigma_{j}w_{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{-}\textnormal{ for every }j\in\{0,...,r\},\\
0,&\textnormal{else.}\end{array}\right.

Set

J:\mathbb{C}^{s+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{s+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r+1}\to\mathbb{C},(u,y,w)\mapsto

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\alpha_{0}(u,y,w)V(\alpha_{1}(u,y,w),...,\alpha_{s}(u,y,w)),&|{\alpha_{i}(u,y,w)}|\leq 1\textnormal{ for all }i\in\{1,...,s\},\\
0,&\textnormal{else}.\end{array}\right.

Then
J
is globally subanalytic. Note that
\sigma_{l}z_{l}(t,z)\in\mathbb{C}^{-}
for every
l\in\{0,...,r\}
and
(t,z)\in H
. Therefore

\Phi_{g}(t,z)=J(\eta(t,z),z_{0}(t,z),...,z_{r}(t,z))

for every
(t,z)\in\Lambda_{g}
.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim}}

With Remark 3.10(2) we are done with the proof of Remark 5.79.

\blacksquare

Now we consider restricted log-exp-analytically prepared functions and so we set up the parametric setting from Section 5.1.3.

For the rest of this section we set the following. Let
l,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be with
n=l+m
. Let
w
range over
\mathbb{R}^{l}
,
u
over
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
x
over
\mathbb{R}
. Let
X\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
be definable with
C\subset X
such that
X_{w}
is open for
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
. We suppose that
\log(E)
consists only of restricted log-exp-analytic functions in
(u,x)
with reference set
X
which are locally bounded in
(u,x)
with reference set
X
. So
f:C\to\mathbb{R},(w,u,x)\mapsto f(w,u,x),
is
(m+1,X)
-restricted log-exp-analytically
(e,r)
-prepared in
x
and every
g\in\log(E)
is
(m+1,X)
-restricted log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared in
x
for an
l\in\{0,...,e-1\}
.

We say ”consistent” respectively ”regular” instead of ”
C
-consistent in
u
with respect to
X
” respectively ”
C
-regular in
u
with respect to
X
”. Let
k:=k^{\textnormal{ch}}
. We fix
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k,r\}
if
k\geq 0
.

The expression ”
\kappa
-persistent” respectively ”
\kappa
-soft” means always ”
(C,\mathcal{Y},\kappa)
-persistent in
u
with respect to
X
” respectively ”
(C,\mathcal{Y},\kappa)
-soft in
u
with respect to
X
”.

We start our considerations with some examples: It may happen that there is an unbounded
g\in\log(E)
such that
\Phi_{g}
has bounded imaginary part or there can be a bounded
g\in\log(E)
such that
\Phi_{g}
is unbounded and has a bounded imaginary part.

5.80 Example

Let
w=0
. Let
X=C:=\textnormal{}]0,1[^{2}
. Let
E:=\{C\to\mathbb{R},(u,x)\mapsto\mathrm{exp}(u\log(x))\}
. Then
E
is a set of one positive definable function with
\log(E)=\{g\}
where
g:C\to\mathbb{R},(u,x)\mapsto u\log(x),
is locally bounded in
(u,x)
with reference set
X
, but not bounded. Consider

f:C\to\mathbb{R},(u,x)\mapsto\mathrm{exp}(u\log(x))

Then
f
is
(2,X)
-restricted log-exp-analytically
(1,1)
-prepared in
x
with center
0
(since
f
is
(1,1)
-prepared in
x
with respect to
E
, the underlying logarithmic scale is
\mathcal{Y}=(x,\log(x))
and
v_{f}=v_{g}=1
). Note that
\mu_{0}=0
and
\mu_{1}=1
. Therefore
H=\textnormal{]}0,1[\times(\mathbb{C}^{-}_{0}\cap\mathbb{C}^{+}_{1})
. We see that
\Lambda=\Lambda_{g}=H
(since
\textnormal{Im}(u\log(z))<\pi
for
(u,z)\in H
),
\Phi_{g}:\Lambda\to\mathbb{C},(u,z)\mapsto u\log(z),
and

F:\Lambda\to\mathbb{C},(u,z)\mapsto\mathrm{exp}(u\log(z)).

Note that
\Phi_{g}
has bounded imaginary part, but is unbounded at zero. Let

D:=\{(u,x)\in\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\times\textnormal{}\mathbb{R}_{>0}\mid e^{-1/u}<x<1\}

and consider
h:=f|_{D}
. Then
h
is
(2,X)
-restricted log-exp-analytically
(1,1)
-prepared in
x
since
h
is log-exp-analytically
(1,1)
-prepared in
x
with respect to
E|_{D}
. Note that
\log(E|_{D})=\{g|_{D}\}
is a set of one bounded function. We have
\Lambda_{h}=\Lambda_{g|_{D}}=\Lambda
and
\Phi_{h}=F
(since
v_{h}=v_{g|_{D}}=1
and therefore
V_{h}=V_{g|_{D}}=1
).

In the following we establish the desired preparation result. The proof is very sophisticated and needs some more preparations.

For a definable function
T:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
we set

T_{\textnormal{pers}}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}1/T(t),&T(t)\neq 0,\\
0,&\textnormal{else,}\end{array}\right.

and

T_{\textnormal{soft}}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}1/T(t),&T(t)\neq 0,\\
1,&\textnormal{else.}\end{array}\right.

Note that
T_{\textnormal{pers}}
and
T_{\textnormal{soft}}
are regular if
T
is regular.

5.81 Proposition

Let
T:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be a regular
\kappa
-persistent function. Let
q\in\mathbb{Q}^{r+1}
be with
(q_{\kappa},...,q_{r})\neq 0
. The following holds.

(1)

Let
q_{\kappa}>0
. Then there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

(2)

Let
k\geq 0
. Suppose that
\kappa=k
and let
q
be
k
-positive with
q_{k}=0
. Suppose that
T_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent. Then there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

(3)

Suppose that
T_{\textnormal{soft}}
is
\kappa
-soft and
q_{\kappa}<0
. Then there is a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T).

Proof

We may assume that
C
is near with respect to
\mu_{\kappa}
. Otherwise we are done with Remark 5.46 by choosing
M=0
in (1) respectively (2) and
N=M=0
in (3). Note that if
\kappa=k
then
\sigma_{k+1}=-1
and
\sigma_{k+2}=...=\sigma_{r}=1
and
\Theta_{\kappa+1}=...=\Theta_{r}=0
by Proposition 5.47 and therefore by definition of
H

\log_{l}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))=\log_{l-1}(-\log(\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z)))\in\mathbb{C}^{-}

for
(t,z)\in H
and
l\in\{1,...,r-k\}
. By Proposition 5.18 we have

\prod_{j=0}^{r}|{z_{j}(t,z)}|^{q_{j}}=\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}|{\sigma_{j}\mathcal{P}_{j,\kappa}(t,z)+e^{\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}}|^{q_{j}}\prod_{l=\kappa}^{r}|{z_{l}(t,z)}|^{q_{l}}

for
(t,z)\in H
. We have
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
if

\prod_{l=\kappa}^{r}|{z_{l}(t,z)}|^{q_{l}}<T(t)\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}\frac{e^{-q_{j}\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}}{|{\frac{\sigma_{j}\mathcal{P}_{j,\kappa}(t,z)}{e^{\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}}+1}|^{q_{j}}}

for
(t,z)\in H
. By Proposition 5.67(1) there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that for every
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M^{+})
and
j\in\{0,...,\kappa-1\}
we have

\bigl|\frac{\sigma_{j}\mathcal{P}_{j,\kappa}(t,z)}{e^{\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}}\bigl|<1/2.

So we have
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
if

\prod_{l=\kappa}^{r}|{z_{l}(t,z)}|^{q_{l}}<\rho T(t)\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}e^{-q_{j}\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}\textnormal{ }(+)

for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M^{+})
where
\rho:=\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}(1/3)^{|{q_{j}}|}
.

Set

\phi:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\rho T(t)\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}e^{-q_{j}\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}.

Note that
\phi
is regular by Example 5.56(1) and Remark 5.55(2) and
\kappa
-persistent by Remark 5.42(1) (compare also with Example 5.43).

To be able to do necessary estimations with the iterated logarithms in
(+)
and to construct the desired functions
M
respectively
N
in the appropriate cases below we need the following Claim 1.

Claim 1

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
z\in\mathbb{C}^{-}
with
\mathrm{exp}_{l}(m^{2}\pi)<|{z}|
. Then
|{\log_{l}(z)}|<\sqrt[2^{l}m]{|{z}|}
.

Proof of Claim 1

For
p\in\mathbb{N}
we set
c_{p}:=\mathrm{exp}(p^{2}\pi)
. We do an induction on
l
.

l=1
: Assume
c_{m}<|{z}|
. We show

\sqrt{|{z}|^{-1/m}(\log^{2}(|{z}|)+\mathrm{arg}^{2}(z))}<1.

One sees that

[c_{m},\infty[\textnormal{}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},x\mapsto\log^{2}(x)x^{-1/m},

is strictly monotone decreasing and that the function

\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},x\mapsto x^{4}\pi^{2}/\mathrm{exp}(x\pi),

takes its values on
]0,1/2[
. Therefore

\log^{2}(c_{m})c_{m}^{-1/m}=\log^{2}(c_{m})/\mathrm{exp}(m\pi)=m^{4}\pi^{2}/\mathrm{exp}(m\pi)<1/2

and consequently

\log^{2}(|{z}|)|{z}|^{-1/m}<1/2.

Clearly

\textnormal{arg}^{2}(z)|{z}|^{-1/m}<1/2.

l-1\to l
: Assume
\mathrm{exp}_{l}(m^{2}\pi)<|{z}|
. We have by the definition of the complex logarithm

c_{1}\leq c_{m}<\log_{l-1}(|{z}|)\leq|{\log_{l-1}(z)}|.

Therefore by the base case for
m=1
applied to
\log_{l-1}(z)

|{\log_{l}(z)}|=|{\log(\log_{l-1}(z))}|<\sqrt{|{\log_{l-1}(z)}|}

and by the inductive hypothesis

\sqrt{|{\log_{l-1}(z)}|}<\sqrt{\sqrt[2^{l-1}m]{|{z}|}}=\sqrt[2^{l}m]{|{z}|}.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }1}

If
k\geq 0
we set

m:=\max\{\lceil{|{q_{j}/q_{b}}|}\rceil\mid j\in\{b+1,...,r\}\}

where
b=k
if
q_{k}\neq 0
or
b=l_{\min}(q)
if
q_{k}=0
.

(1): Suppose that
q_{\kappa}>0
. We do two cases.

Case 1
:

Assume
\kappa=r
or if
\kappa=k
(i.e.
k\geq 0
) then
q_{k+1}=...=q_{r}=0
. Let

\hat{M}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\sqrt[q_{\kappa}]{\phi(t)}.

Then
\hat{M}
is
\kappa
-persistent by Remark 5.42(2) and regular by Remark 5.55(2). If
|{z_{\kappa}(t,z)}|<\hat{M}(t)
then
|{z_{\kappa}(t,z)}|^{q_{\kappa}}<\phi(t)
for
(t,z)\in H
(so the inequality in
(+)
is fulfilled for such a
(t,z)
). By Proposition 5.67(1) there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M^{*}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M^{*})\subset\mathcal{B}(z_{\kappa},\hat{M})
. So take
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{M^{*}(t),M^{+}(t)\}
. Clearly
M
is regular by Remark 5.55(2) and
\kappa
-persistent by Remark 5.42(3). With
(+)
we have
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

Case 2
:

Assume
\kappa=k
(i.e.
k\geq 0
),
q_{k}>0
and
(q_{k+1},...,q_{r})\neq 0
. Set

\hat{M}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{\sqrt[s]{\phi(t)},1/\mathrm{exp}_{r-k}(m^{2}\pi)\},

where
s:=\frac{q_{k}}{2^{r-k}}
. Then
\hat{M}
is regular and
k
-persistent.
Let
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(z_{k},\hat{M})
. We show that the estimation in
(+)
holds. We have

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}|{z_{k}(t,z)}|^{-\tfrac{1}{2^{j}}}<\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}

and therefore

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}(1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|)^{\tfrac{1}{2^{j}}}<\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}.

We obtain

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}(1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|)^{\bigl|\tfrac{q_{k+j}}{2^{j}mq_{k}}\bigl|}<\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}

since
1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|>\mathrm{exp}_{r-k}(m^{2}\pi)
and
|{\tfrac{q_{k+j}}{mq_{k}}}|\leq 1
. With Claim 1 applied to
1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z)
we obtain

|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|<\sqrt[2^{j}m]{1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|}

for
j\in\{1,...,r-k\}
and therefore

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|^{|{q_{k+j}/q_{k}}|}<\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}.

Because
|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|>1
for every
j\in\{1,...,r-k\}
we obtain

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|^{q_{k+j}/q_{k}}<\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}.

Now define
M
outgoing from
\hat{M}
in the same way as in Case 1. With
(+)
we have
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,k},M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

(2): Suppose that
\kappa=k
, that
q
is
k
-positive with
q_{k}=0
and that
T_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent. We have that
\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent by Example 5.51(2), Remark 5.52(1)/(3) (since
T_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent). Let
l:=l_{\min}(q)
. Note that
q_{l}<0
. Set

\hat{M}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\min\{1/\mathrm{exp}_{l-k}(\sqrt[s]{\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}(t)}),1/\mathrm{exp}_{r-k}(m^{2}\pi)\},&\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}(t)\neq 0,\\
0,&\textnormal{else,}\end{array}\right.

where
s:=-(1/2)^{r-l}q_{l}
. Since
\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent we obtain that
\sqrt[s]{\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}}
is consistent by Remark 5.52(2). By Remark 5.52(3) we see that
\mathrm{exp}_{j}(\sqrt[s]{\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}})
is also consistent for
j\in\{1,...,l-k\}
. So we see that
\hat{M}
is regular by Remark 5.55(2).

Claim 2

\hat{M}
is
\kappa
-persistent.

Proof of Claim 2

Let
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to C_{w}
be a definable curve compatible with
C_{w}
with

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma(y)\in X_{w}
and

\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))=0.

Since
\phi
is
\kappa
-persistent we see
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\phi(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0
and therefore

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }2}

Let
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(z_{k},\hat{M})
. Then
\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}(t)\neq 0
. We show that the inequality in
(+)
is satisfied for this
(t,z)
. Let
y:=\log_{l-k}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))
. Note that

\max\{\sqrt[s]{\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}(t)},\mathrm{exp}_{r-l}(m^{2}\pi)\}<|{y}|.

We obtain with
\phi_{\textnormal{pers}}(t)=1/\phi(t)

|{y}|\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}|{y}|^{-\tfrac{1}{2^{j-l}}}>\sqrt[q_{l}]{\phi(t)}

and therefore

|{y}|\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}(1/|{y}|)^{\tfrac{1}{2^{j-l}}}>\sqrt[q_{l}]{\phi(t)}.

This gives

|{y}|\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}(1/|{y}|)^{\bigl|\tfrac{q_{j}}{2^{j-l}mq_{l}}\bigl|}>\sqrt[q_{l}]{\phi(t)}

since
|{y}|>1
and
|{\tfrac{q_{j}}{mq_{l}}}|\leq 1
. Since
\mathrm{exp}_{r-l}(m^{2}\pi)<|{y}|
we obtain with Claim 1 applied to
y

|{\log_{j-l}(y)}|<\sqrt[2^{j-l}m]{|{y}|}

for
j\in\{l+1,...,r\}
and therefore

|{y}|\prod_{j=l+1}^{r}|{\log_{j-l}(y)}|^{-|{q_{j}/q_{l}}|}>\sqrt[q_{l}]{\phi(t)}.

Because
|{\log_{j-l}(y)}|>1
for every
j\in\{l+1,...,r\}
we obtain

\prod_{j=l}^{r}|{\log_{j-k}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|^{q_{j}/q_{l}}>\sqrt[q_{l}]{\phi(t)}

and therefore

\prod_{j=l}^{r}|{\log_{j-k}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|^{q_{j}}<\phi(t).

Now define
M
outgoing from
\hat{M}
in the same way as in
(1)
in Case 1. With
(+)
we have
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,k},M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

(3): Suppose that
T_{\textnormal{soft}}
is
\kappa
-soft and
q_{\kappa}<0
. We see with Remark 5.52(3) that
\phi_{\textnormal{soft}}
is also
\kappa
-soft. We have to consider two more cases.

Case 1
:

Assume
\kappa=r
or if
\kappa=k
then
q_{k+1}=...=q_{r}=0
. Let

\hat{N}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\sqrt[-q_{\kappa}]{\phi_{\textnormal{soft}}(t)}.

Then
\hat{N}
is regular and
\kappa
-soft. For
(t,z)\in H
with
\hat{N}(t)<|{z_{\kappa}(t,z)}|<1
we see that the inequality in
(+)
is satisfied (since
\phi(t)\neq 0
). By Proposition 5.67(2) there is a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M^{*}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M^{*})\subset\mathcal{A}(z_{\kappa},\hat{N},1)
. So take
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{M^{*}(t),M^{+}(t)\}
. With (+) we see that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

Case 2:

Assume
\kappa=k
and
(q_{k+1},...,q_{r})\neq 0
. Let
s:=-(2-(1/2)^{r-k})q_{k}
. Set

\hat{N}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{>0},t\mapsto\sqrt[s]{\phi_{\textnormal{soft}}(t)},

and

\hat{M}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{>0},t\mapsto 1/\mathrm{exp}_{r-k}(m^{2}\pi).

Then
\hat{N}
is
k
-soft and regular.
Let
(t,z)\in\mathcal{A}(z_{\kappa},\hat{N},\hat{M})
. Then
\phi(t)\neq 0
. We show that the inequality in
(+)
is satisfied for this
(t,z)
. We obtain

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}|{z_{k}(t,z)}|^{\tfrac{1}{2^{j}}}>\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}

and therefore

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}(1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|)^{-\tfrac{1}{2^{j}}}>\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}.

We obtain

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}(1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|)^{-\bigl|\tfrac{q_{k+j}}{2^{j}mq_{k}}\bigl|}>\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}

since
1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|>\mathrm{exp}_{r-k}(m^{2}\pi)
and
|{\tfrac{q_{k+j}}{mq_{k}}}|\leq 1
. With Claim 1 applied to
1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z)
we obtain

|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|<\sqrt[2^{j}m]{1/|{z_{k}(t,z)}|}

for
j\in\{1,...,r-k\}
and therefore

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|^{-|{q_{k+j}/q_{k}}|}>\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}.

Because
|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|>1
for every
j\in\{1,...,r-k\}
we obtain

|{z_{k}(t,z)}|\prod_{j=1}^{r-k}|{\log_{j}(1/\sigma_{k}z_{k}(t,z))}|^{q_{k+j}/q_{k}}>\sqrt[q_{k}]{\phi(t)}.

Now define
N
and
M
analoguously as in Case 1 (outgoing from
\hat{N}
and
\hat{M}
instead from
\hat{N}
and
1
). With
(+)
we have
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

All in all we are done with the proof of Proposition 5.81.

\blacksquare

5.82 Corollary

Let
T:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}
be
\kappa
-persistent. Suppose that
T_{\textnormal{soft}}
is
\kappa
-soft. Let
q\in\mathbb{Q}^{r+1}
be
\kappa
-negative. Then there is a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that

\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T).

Proof

Note that
\sqrt{T}
is
\kappa
-persistent and that
\sqrt{T_{\textnormal{soft}}}
is
\kappa
-soft. Let
q_{\kappa}^{*}:=|{q_{\kappa}}|+1
. Let

\hat{\mathcal{Z}}:=(\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa})^{q_{\kappa}-q_{\kappa}^{*}}\prod_{j\neq\kappa}(\sigma_{j}z_{j})^{q_{j}}.

Then we have
\mathcal{B}((\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa})^{q_{\kappa}^{*}},\sqrt{T})\cap\mathcal{B}(\hat{\mathcal{Z}},\sqrt{T})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
. With Proposition 5.81(1) respectively Proposition 5.81(3) we find regular
\kappa
-persistent functions
M_{1},M_{2}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-soft function
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that

\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M_{1})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa})^{q^{*}_{\kappa}},\sqrt{T})
and
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M_{2})\subset\mathcal{B}(\hat{\mathcal{Z}},\sqrt{T})
. Set
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{M_{1}(t),M_{2}(t)\}.
We see that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q},T)
.

\blacksquare

5.83 Convention

Let
D\subset\pi(C)
be definable. If a function
g:D\to\mathbb{R}
is the restriction of a regular (consistent) function then we call
g
also regular (consistent).

For the next proposition we set the following: For a definable function
W:Y\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
where
Y\subset\pi(C)
is definable we set

W^{C}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}W(t),&t\in Y,\\
0,&\textnormal{ else.}\end{array}\right.

For
0<l\leq m
we denote by
M_{l}(s\times(r+1),\mathbb{Q})
the set of all
(s\times(r+1))
-matrices whose first
l
columns are zero.

Now we are ready to establish the desired preparation result.

5.84 Proposition

Let
g\in\log(E)\cup\{f\}
. There is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)\subset\Lambda_{g}
and
G:=\Phi_{g}|_{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with a complex LA-preparing tuple

(r,\mathcal{Z},a_{G},q_{G},s_{G},V_{G},b_{G},P_{G})

where
q_{G}:=((q_{G})_{0},...,(q_{G})_{r})
,
b_{G}:=(b_{1,G},...,b_{s_{G},G})
and the following holds.

(1)

We have
(q_{G})_{0},...,(q_{G})_{\kappa-1}=0
and
P_{G}\in M_{\kappa}(s\times(r+1),\mathbb{Q})
.

(2)

a_{G}
is regular. If
g\in\log(E)
then the following holds:
a_{G}
is consistent and if
q_{G}
is
\kappa
-negative then
1/((|{a_{G}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft.

(3)

b_{G}
is regular, consistent and if
p_{i,G}
is
\kappa
-negative for
i\in\{1,...,s_{G}\}
then
1/((|{b_{i,G}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft where
P_{G}:=(p_{1,G},...,p_{s_{G},G})^{t}
.

Proof

Let
g
be log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared in
x
with respect to
E
for
l\in\{-1,...,e\}
. We do an induction on
l
. For
l=-1
there is nothing to show.

l-1\to l:
Let

(r,\mathcal{Y},a,e^{d_{0}},q,s,v,b,e^{d},P):=(r,\mathcal{Y},a_{g},e^{d_{0,g}},q_{g},s,v_{g},b_{g},e^{d_{g}},P_{g})

where
b_{g}:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
,
\mathrm{exp}(d_{g}):=(\mathrm{exp}(d_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(d_{s}))
,
P_{g}:=(p_{1},...,p_{s})^{t}
and
|{v_{g}}|<\pi
. We may assume that
a\neq 0
. Otherwise the statement is clear. Note that
d_{i}
is locally bounded in
(u,x)
with reference set
X
for every
i\in\{0,...,s\}
. For
i\in\{0,...,s\}
let
\Lambda_{i}:=\Lambda_{d_{i}}
. Let

\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{C}}=(r,\mathcal{Z},a,\mathrm{exp}(\tilde{D}_{0}),q,s,V,b,\mathrm{exp}(\tilde{D}),P)

be a complex preparing tuple for
\Phi_{g}
where
\tilde{D}_{j}:=\Phi_{d_{j}}
for
j\in\{0,...,s\}
and
\mathrm{exp}(\tilde{D}):=(\mathrm{exp}(\tilde{D}_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(\tilde{D}_{s}))
.

By the inductive hypothesis there is a regular
\kappa
-soft
N^{*}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M^{*}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that for
i\in\{0,...,s\}

B^{*}:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N^{*},M^{*})\subset\Lambda_{i}

and
D_{i}:=\tilde{D}_{i}|_{B^{*}}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with complex LA-preparing tuple

(r,\mathcal{Z},a_{D_{i}},q_{D_{i}},s_{D_{i}},V_{D_{i}},b_{D_{i}},P_{D_{i}})

where
q_{D_{i}}:=((q_{D_{i}})_{0},...,(q_{D_{i}})_{r})
,
P_{D_{i}}:=(p_{1,D_{i}},...,p_{s_{D_{i}},D_{i}})^{t}
,
b_{D_{i}}:=(b_{1,D_{i}},...,b_{s_{D_{i}},D_{i}})
and the following properties (1) - (3) holds for this preparing tuple:

(1)

(q_{D_{i}})_{0}=...=(q_{D_{i}})_{\kappa-1}=0
and
P_{D_{i}}\in M_{\kappa}(s_{D_{i}}\times(r+1),\mathbb{Q})
.

(2)

a_{D_{i}}
is regular. If
g\in\log(E)
the following holds:
a_{D_{i}}
is consistent and if
q_{D_{i}}
is
\kappa
-negative then
1/((|{a_{D_{i}}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft.

(3)

b_{D_{i}}
is regular, consistent and if
p_{j,D_{i}}
is
\kappa
-negative then
1/((|{b_{j,D_{i}}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft for
j\in\{1,...,s_{D_{i}}\}
.

At first we deal with
\mathrm{exp}(D_{0}),...,\mathrm{exp}(D_{s})
and start with the following

Claim 1

Let
0<\delta<1/2
. Then there is a regular
\kappa
-soft
S_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
T_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with
Y_{\delta}:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},S_{\delta},T_{\delta})\subset B^{*}
and for
i\in\{0,...,s\}
definable functions
D_{i,1}:\pi(Y_{\delta})\to\mathbb{R},t\mapsto D_{i,1}(t),
and
D_{i,2}:Y_{\delta}\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto D_{i,2}(t,z),
with the following properties.

(1)*

D_{i}|_{Y_{\delta}}=D_{i,1}+D_{i,2}
.

(2)*

D_{i,1}
is regular and consistent.

(3)*

D_{i,2}
is bounded by
\delta
. Additionally
\mathrm{exp}(D_{i,2})
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with complex LA-preparing tuple
(r,\mathcal{Z},a^{*},q^{*},s^{*},V^{*},b^{*},P^{*})
with which fulfills properties (1) - (3) above where
a^{*}=1
and
q^{*}=0
.

Proof of Claim 1

Let
K\in\{D_{0},...,D_{s}\}
. It suffices to find a regular
\kappa
-soft
\hat{S}_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\hat{T}_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with
W:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{S}_{\delta},\hat{T}_{\delta})\subset B^{*}
and corresponding functions
K_{1}:\pi(W)\to\mathbb{R}
and
K_{2}:W\to\mathbb{C}
which fulfill properties (1)* - (3)*. Let

(r,\mathcal{Z},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{s},\mathfrak{V},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{P}):=(r,\mathcal{Z},a_{K},q_{K},s_{K},V_{K},b_{K},P_{K}).

Suppose
\mathfrak{a}\neq 0
. Otherwise the claim follows. Fix
R>1
such that
\mathfrak{V}
converges absolutely on
\overline{D^{\mathfrak{s}}(0,R)}
. Let
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathfrak{s}}}c_{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}
be the power series expansion of
\mathfrak{V}
where
Z:=(Z_{1},...,Z_{s})
. Fix
L\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
with
\delta<L
such that
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathfrak{s}}}|{c_{\alpha}w^{\alpha}}|<L
for
w\in D^{\mathfrak{s}}(0,R)
. We are doing two cases.

1. Case:

(\mathfrak{q}_{\kappa},...,\mathfrak{q}_{r})\neq 0
.

Consider the regular function

T_{\delta}^{*}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\min\{\frac{\delta}{L|{\mathfrak{a}(t)}|},\frac{1}{|{\mathfrak{a}(t)}|}\},&t\in\pi(B^{*}),\\
0,&\textnormal{else}.\end{array}\right.

Subclaim 1

T_{\delta}^{*}
is
\kappa
-persistent.

Proof of Subclaim 1

Let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Let
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to C_{w}
be a definable curve compatible with
C_{w}
with
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma(y)\in X_{w}
and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|=0
. Note that there is
0<\epsilon\leq 1
such that
(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\pi(B^{*})
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[
(since
N^{*}
is
\kappa
-soft and
M^{*}
is
\kappa
-persistent). Since
\mathfrak{a}
is consistent we see that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}T_{\delta}^{*}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Subclaim }1}

Note that if
|{(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{q}}(t,z)}|<T_{\delta}^{*}(t)
then
|{K(t,z)}|<\delta
for
(t,z)\in B^{*}
.

1.1 Case:

\mathfrak{q}
is
\kappa
-positive.

By Proposition 5.81(1) if
q_{\kappa}>0
respectively Proposition 5.81(2) if
\kappa=k\geq 0
and
q_{k}=0
(since
(T_{\delta}^{*})_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent, because
\mathfrak{a}
is consistent) there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M_{\delta}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},M_{\delta}^{+})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{q}},T_{\delta}^{*})
. Take
\hat{S}_{\delta}:=N^{*}
,

\hat{T}_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{M_{\delta}^{+}(t),M^{*}(t)\},

K_{1}:=0
and
K_{2}:=K|_{W}
where
W:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{S}_{\delta},\hat{T}_{\delta})
. Note that
\mathrm{exp}(K_{2})=\mathrm{exp}^{*}(K_{2})
where

\mathrm{exp}^{*}:\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C},z\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{exp}(z),&|{z}|\leq\delta,\\
0,&\textnormal{ else,}\end{array}\right.

is globally subanalytic. Additionally we obtain
|{\mathfrak{a}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{q}}(t,z)}|<1
for
(t,z)\in W
. By composition of power series we get the desired preparation for
\mathrm{exp}(K_{2})
.

1.2 Case:

\mathfrak{q}
is
\kappa
-negative.

Similarly as in the proof of Subclaim 1 we see that
(T_{\delta}^{*})_{\textnormal{soft}}
is
\kappa
-soft (since
1/((|{\mathfrak{a}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft). By Corollary 5.82 there is a regular
\kappa
-soft
N_{\delta}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M_{\delta}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N_{\delta}^{+},M_{\delta}^{+})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{q}},T_{\delta}^{*})
. Take
\hat{S}_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\max\{N_{\delta}^{+}(t),N^{*}(t)\}
,
\hat{T}_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{M_{\delta}^{+}(t),M^{*}(t)\}
,
K_{1}:=0
and
K_{2}:=K|_{W}
where
W=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{S}_{\delta},\hat{T}_{\delta})
. We are done in a completely similar way as in Case 1.1.

2. Case:

(\mathfrak{q}_{\kappa},...,\mathfrak{q}_{r})=0
.

Let

\mathcal{S}:=\{i\in\{1,...,\mathfrak{s}\}\mid(\mathfrak{p}_{i\kappa},...,\mathfrak{p}_{ir})\neq 0\}.

If
\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
then
K=\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{V}(\mathfrak{b}_{1},...,\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{s}})
is a regular and consistent function considered as function on
\pi(B^{*})
. So we are done by taking
\hat{S}_{\delta}:=N^{*}
,
\hat{T}_{\delta}:=M^{*}
,
K_{1}:=0
and
K_{2}:=K
. So suppose
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
. Let

\Gamma_{1}:=\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\mid{{}^{t}}P\alpha=0\}

and
\Gamma_{2}:=\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathfrak{s}}\setminus\Gamma_{1}.
Note that
\Gamma_{2}\neq\emptyset
. Set
\mathfrak{V}_{1}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{1}}c_{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}
and
\mathfrak{V}_{2}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{2}}c_{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}
. For
l\in\{1,2\}
let

J_{l}:=\mathfrak{a}\mathfrak{V}_{l}(\mathfrak{b}_{1}(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{p}_{1}},...,\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{s}}(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{s}}}).

Note that
K=J_{1}+J_{2}
. Let
\phi_{i}:=\mathfrak{b}_{i}(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
i\in\{1,...,\mathfrak{s}\}
. For
j\in\mathcal{S}
we define the regular function

U_{j,\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\bigl|\frac{\delta}{L\mathfrak{a}(t)\mathfrak{b}_{j}(t)}\bigl|,&t\in\pi(B^{*}),\\
0,&\textnormal{else}.\end{array}\right.

Similarly as in the proof of Subclaim 1 one sees for
j\in\mathcal{S}
that
U_{j,\delta}
is
\kappa
-persistent (since
\mathfrak{b}_{j}
and
\mathfrak{a}
are consistent) and that if
\mathfrak{p}_{j}
is
\kappa
-negative then
(U_{j,\delta})_{\textnormal{soft}}
is
\kappa
-soft (since
\mathfrak{a}
is consistent and
1/((|{\mathfrak{b}_{j}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft). Additionally
(U_{j,\delta})_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent for
j\in\mathcal{S}
.

We see that for
j\in\mathcal{S}
there is a regular
\kappa
-soft
\hat{S}_{j,\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\hat{T}_{j,\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that

\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{S}_{j,\delta},\hat{T}_{j,\delta})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\mathfrak{p}_{j}},U_{j,\delta}).

(By Proposition 5.81(1) if
\mathfrak{p}_{j}
is
\kappa
-positive and
\mathfrak{p}_{j\kappa}\neq 0
respectively Proposition 5.81(2) if
\mathfrak{p}_{j}
is
\kappa
-positive,
\kappa=k\geq 0
and
\mathfrak{p}_{jk}=0
since
(U_{j,\delta})_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent respectively Corollary 5.82 if
\mathfrak{p}_{j}
is
\kappa
-negative. If
\mathfrak{p}_{j}
is
\kappa
-positive take
\hat{S}_{j,\delta}=0
.)
Of course we may assume that
N^{*}\leq\hat{S}_{j,\delta}
and
\hat{T}_{j,\delta}\leq M^{*}
for
j\in\mathcal{S}
. Consider
\hat{S}_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\max\{\hat{S}_{j,\delta}(t)\mid j\in\mathcal{S}\}
and
\hat{T}_{\delta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{\hat{T}_{j,\delta}(t)\mid j\in\mathcal{S}\}
. Then
\hat{S}_{\delta}
is regular and
\kappa
-soft and
\hat{T}_{\delta}
is regular and
\kappa
-persistent. Consider
W:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{S}_{\delta},\hat{T}_{\delta})
.

To obtain Claim 1 it remains to show that
\mathrm{exp}(J_{2})|_{W}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
as desired (see Subclaim 2) and that
J_{1}|_{W}
coincides with a regular and consistent function on
\pi(W)
.

Subclaim 2

For
(t,z)\in W
we have
|{J_{2}(t,z)}|<\delta
. The function
\mathrm{exp}(J_{2})|_{W}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
as required in property (3)*.

Proof of Subclaim 2

Set
\phi:=(\phi_{1},...,\phi_{\mathfrak{s}})
. For every
\alpha:=(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{\mathfrak{s}})\in\Gamma_{2}
fix
i_{\alpha}\in\{1,...,\mathfrak{s}\}
with
i_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{S}
and
\alpha_{i_{\alpha}}\neq 0
. Therefore we have for
(t,z)\in W

|{\mathfrak{a}(t)\phi_{i_{\alpha}}(t,z)}|<\delta/L.

We obtain

\displaystyle|{J_{2}(t,z)}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|{\mathfrak{a}(t)\mathfrak{V}_{2}(\phi(t,z))}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|{\mathfrak{a}(t)\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{1}}c_{\alpha}\phi_{i_{\alpha}}(t,z)^{\alpha_{i_{\alpha}}}\prod_{j\neq i_{\alpha}}\phi_{j}(t,z)^{\alpha_{j}}}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|{\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{1}}c_{\alpha}\mathfrak{a}(t)\phi_{i_{\alpha}}(t,z)\phi_{i_{\alpha}}(t,z)^{\alpha_{i_{\alpha}}-1}\prod_{j\neq i_{\alpha}}\phi_{j}(t,z)^{\alpha_{j}}}|

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\delta/L\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{1}}|{c_{\alpha}\phi_{i_{\alpha}}(t,z)^{\alpha_{i_{\alpha}}-1}\prod_{j\neq i_{\alpha}}\phi_{j}(t,z)^{\alpha_{j}}}|

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\delta.

Let
\mathcal{S}:=\{j_{1},...,j_{\beta}\}
where
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that
i_{\alpha}\in\{j_{1},...,j_{\beta}\}
is unique for
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathfrak{s}}
. Let
(w_{j_{1}},...,w_{j_{\beta}})
be a new tuple of complex variables. Consider for
\gamma\in\{1,...,\beta\}

\tilde{\phi}_{\gamma}:W\to\overline{B(0,1)},(t,z)\mapsto a(t)b_{j_{\gamma}}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{p}_{j_{\gamma}}}(t,z).

Consider

\hat{V}:\overline{D^{\mathfrak{s}+\beta}(0,1)}\to\mathbb{C},(z_{1},...,z_{s},w_{j_{1}},...,w_{j_{\beta}})\mapsto\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{2}}c_{\alpha}w_{i_{\alpha}}z_{i_{\alpha}}^{\alpha_{i_{\alpha}}-1}\prod_{j\neq i_{\alpha}}z_{j}^{\alpha_{j}}.

Note that
\hat{V}
defines a power series which converges absolutely on an open neighbourhood of
\overline{D^{\mathfrak{s}+\beta}(0,1)}
(and is therefore globally subanalytic). We have for
(t,z)\in W

J_{2}(t,z)=\hat{V}(\phi_{1}(t,z),...,\phi_{s}(t,z),\tilde{\phi}_{1}(t,z),...,\tilde{\phi}_{\beta}(t,z))

and therefore

\mathrm{exp}(J_{2}(t,z))=\mathrm{exp}^{*}(\hat{V}(\phi_{1}(t,z),...,\phi_{\mathfrak{s}}(t,z),\tilde{\phi}_{1}(t,z),...,\tilde{\phi}_{\beta}(t,z)))

where

\mathrm{exp}^{*}:\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C},z\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{exp}(z),&|{z}|\leq\delta,\\
0,&\textnormal{else}.\end{array}\right.

By using the exponential series and composition of power series we see that
\mathrm{exp}(J_{2})
has the desired properties.
\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Subclaim }2}

Set

\Xi:\pi(W)\to\mathbb{R},t\mapsto\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{1}}\mathfrak{a}(t)c_{\alpha}\prod_{j=1}^{\mathfrak{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{j}(t)^{\alpha_{j}}.

Then we obtain

J_{1}(t,z)=\Xi(t)
for
(t,z)\in W
. We show that
\Xi
is consistent and regular and are done with Claim 1. Note that

\Xi(t)=\mathfrak{a}(t)\mathfrak{V}_{1}(\mathfrak{b}_{1}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{p}_{1}}(t,\lambda(t)),...,\mathfrak{b}_{\mathfrak{s}}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{s}}}(t,\lambda(t)))

for
t\in\pi(W)
where

\lambda:\pi(W)\to\mathbb{R},t\mapsto\mu_{\kappa}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa}\sigma_{j})\frac{\hat{S}_{\delta}(t)+\hat{T}_{\delta}(t)}{2},

is regular. (Note also that
(t,\lambda(t))\in W
for
t\in\pi(W)
.) So we see that
\Xi
is consistent since
\mathfrak{a}
is consistent.

Subclaim 3

The function
\Xi
is regular.

Proof of Subclaim 3

Similarly as in the proof of the claim in Remark 5.79 we find a globally subanalytic function
G:\mathbb{C}^{s+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r-\kappa+1}\to\mathbb{C}
such that for
t\in\pi(W)

\Xi(t)=G(\eta(t),\mathcal{Z}_{\kappa}(t,\lambda(t)))

where
\mathcal{Z}_{\kappa}:=(z_{\kappa},...,z_{r})
and
\eta:\pi(W)\to\mathbb{R}^{s+1},t\mapsto(\mathfrak{a}(t),\mathfrak{b}_{1}(t),...,\mathfrak{b}_{s}(t))
. Note that
\eta
and
\Theta
are regular. By Remark 5.55(2) it is enough to show that
\pi(C)\to\mathbb{C},t\mapsto z_{l}(t,\lambda(t)),
is regular. We do an induction on
l\in\{0,...,r\}
.

l=0
: We have
z_{0}(t,\lambda(t))=\lambda(t)-\Theta_{0}(t)
for
t\in\pi(W)
. Remark 5.55(2) gives the result since
\Theta_{0}
is regular.

l-1\to l
: We have
z_{l}(t,\lambda(t))=\log(\sigma_{l-1}z_{l-1}(t,\lambda(t)))-\Theta_{l}(t)
for
t\in\pi(W)
. Remark 5.55(2) and the inductive hypothesis gives the result since
\Theta_{l}
is regular.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Subclaim }3}

So we set
K_{1}:=\Xi
and
K_{2}:=J_{2}|_{W}
.

All in all we obtain Claim 1.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }1}

Let
Y_{\delta}
and
D_{i,j}
as in Claim 1 for

. We obtain for

V(b_{1}(t)\mathrm{exp}(D_{1,1}(t))(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{1}}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{1,2}(t,z)),...,

b_{s}(t)\mathrm{exp}(D_{s,1}(t))(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{s}}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{s,2}(t,z)))

Note that

Claim 2

Let
1<\epsilon
and
p_{0}:=(p_{00},...,p_{0r}):=q
. There is a regular

\omega_{i}:=\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}(\sigma_{j}z_{j})^{p_{ij}}|_{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N^{*},T_{\epsilon})}

Proof

Let
\nu\in\{p_{0},...,p_{s}\}
. It suffices to find a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\hat{T}_{\epsilon}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
with
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N^{*},\hat{T}_{\epsilon})\subset B^{*}
such that
\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}(\sigma_{j}z_{j})^{\nu_{j}}|_{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N^{*},\hat{T}_{\epsilon})}

Let
0<\beta\leq 1/2
be such that
1/\epsilon<\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}|{1+z}|^{\nu_{j}}<\epsilon
for

. By Proposition 5.67(1) there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
T^{*}_{\beta}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that

\Bigl|\frac{\mathcal{P}_{j,\kappa}(t,z)}{e^{\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}}\Bigl|<\beta

for every
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},T^{*}_{\beta})
and every

. We have

\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}(\sigma_{j}z_{j}(t,z))^{\nu_{j}}=\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa-1}e^{\nu_{j}\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}\bigl(1+\frac{\sigma_{j}\mathcal{P}_{j,\kappa}(t,z)}{e^{\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}}\bigl)^{\nu_{j}}\textnormal{ }(+)

for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},T^{*}_{\beta})
(compare with Proposition 5.18).

We say that a function
S:Z\to\mathbb{C}
where
Z\subset\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},T^{*}_{\beta})
is definable fulfills property
(*)_{\textnormal{prep}}
if the following holds:

J
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with complex LA-preparing tuple

(r,\mathcal{Z},a_{J},q_{J},s_{J},V_{J},b_{J},P_{J})

such that
a_{J}\neq 0
,
a_{J}
and
b_{J}
are regular and consistent,
(q_{J})_{\kappa+1}=1
and
(q_{J})_{j}=0
for
j\in\{1,...,r+1\}\setminus\{\kappa+1\}
,
(P_{J})_{i(\kappa+1)}=1
and
(P_{J})_{ij}=0
for
j\in\{1,...,r+1\}\setminus\{\kappa+1\}
and
i\in\{1,...,s_{J}\}
.

Let
\Omega:=T_{\beta}^{*}
.

Subclaim 1

By shrinking
\Omega
if necessary we have that
\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa}|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)}
fulfills property
(*)_{\textnormal{prep}}
(i.e. there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent

such that

Proof of Subclaim 1

Let
E_{0}:=z_{\kappa}|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)}
. Inductively for
l\in\{1,...,\kappa\}
let

E_{l}:\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\sigma_{k-l}e^{\mu_{l-1,\kappa}(t)}(e^{E_{l-1}(t,z)}-1).

We see by an easy induction on
l\in\{0,...,\kappa\}
that
E_{l}(t,z)=\mathcal{P}_{\kappa-l,\kappa}(t,z)
for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)
. We show by induction on
l\in\{0,...,\kappa\}
that
E_{l}|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)}
fulfills property
(*)_{\textnormal{prep}}
by shrinking
\Omega
if necessary. For
l=0
this is clear.

l-1\to l:
Let

(r,\mathcal{Z},\tilde{a},\tilde{q},\tilde{s},\tilde{V},\tilde{b},\tilde{P})

be a corresponding complex LA-preparing tuple for
E_{l-1}|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)}
where
\tilde{P}:=(\tilde{p}_{i(j-1)})_{i,j}
. We have for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)

E_{l}(t,z)=\sigma_{k-l}e^{\mu_{l-1,\kappa}(t)}(\mathrm{exp}(\tilde{a}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)Q(t,z))-1)

where

Q(t,z):=\tilde{V}(\tilde{b}_{1}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z),...,\tilde{b}_{\tilde{s}}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)).

We see

E_{l}(t,z)=\sigma_{k-l}e^{\mu_{l-1,\kappa}(t)}\tilde{a}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)\frac{\mathrm{exp}(\tilde{a}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)Q(t,z))-1}{\tilde{a}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)}.

Note that
\tilde{a}\neq 0
. Because
\tilde{a}
is consistent and regular we see that
1/|{\tilde{a}}|
is regular and
\kappa
-persistent. So we may assume that
|{z_{\kappa}(t,z)}|<1/|{\tilde{a}(t)}|
for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)
after shrinking
\Omega
if necessary (by Proposition 5.81(1) there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\hat{M}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{M})\subset\mathcal{B}(z_{\kappa},1/|{\tilde{a}}|)
). Note that
\sigma_{k-l}e^{\mu_{l-1,\kappa}}\tilde{a}
is consistent and regular (since
e^{\mu_{l-1,\kappa}}
is consistent and regular). Now it is easy to check by using the exponential series and composition of power series that
E_{l}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared with a preparing tuple which fulfills the desired properties.
\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Subclaim }1}

Subclaim 2

By shrinking
\Omega
if necessary we have that
\mathcal{P}_{l,\kappa}|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)}
fulfills property
(*)_{\textnormal{prep}}
for

.

Proof of Subclaim 2

We do an induction on
l
. By Claim 1 we get the assertion for
l=0
.

l-1\to l:
Let

(r,\mathcal{Z},\hat{a},\hat{q},\hat{s},\hat{V},\hat{b},\hat{P})

be a corresponding complex LA-preparing tuple for
\mathcal{P}_{l-1,\kappa}|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)}
. Let
\alpha:=\hat{a}/e^{\mu_{\kappa-l,\kappa}}
. We have for
(t,z)\in D

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{l,\kappa}(t,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log^{*}(1+\frac{\mathcal{P}_{l-1,\kappa}(t,z)}{e^{\mu_{\kappa-l,\kappa}(t)}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)\frac{\log^{*}(1+\alpha(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)Q(t,z))}{\alpha(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)}

where for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)

Q(t,z):=\hat{V}(\hat{b}_{1}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z),...,\hat{b}_{\hat{s}}(t)\sigma_{\kappa}z_{\kappa}(t,z)).

Note that
\alpha
is regular and consistent since
e^{\mu_{\kappa-l,\kappa}}
is regular and consistent. So

is regular and
\kappa
-persistent. So by shrinking
\Omega
if necessary we may assume that
|{z_{\kappa}(t,z)}|<1/|{\alpha(t)}|
for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)
. We see by using the logarithmic series and composition of power series that
\mathcal{P}_{l,\kappa}|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\Omega)}
fulfills property
(*)_{\textnormal{prep}}
.
\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Subclaim }2}

So by Subclaim 2 we find for
j\in\{0,...,\kappa-1\}
a complex preparing tuple

(r,\mathcal{Z},a^{*}_{j},q^{*}_{j},s^{*}_{j},V^{*}_{j},b^{*}_{j},P^{*}_{j})

for

according to property
(*)_{\textnormal{prep}}
after shrinking
\Omega
if necessary. Note that

-persistent. So by further shrinking

and

. So choose

. With (+) and composition of power series we obtain the desired preparation for

Claim 3

There is a regular
\kappa

(p_{i\kappa},...,p_{ir})\neq 0
