Since
A_{i}
is consistent we see that
U_{\textnormal{pers}}
is also consistent. So with the proof of Subclaim 1 in Claim 1 we obtain that
U
is
\kappa
-persistent. For
(t,z)\in\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{i}}_{o},U)\cap Y
we have
|{\phi_{i}(t,z)}|<R
since
|{Q_{i}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{i,2}(t,z))}|<\sqrt{R}
.

Suppose that
p_{i}
is
\kappa
-positive. By Proposition 5.81(1) if
p_{i\kappa}\neq 0
respectively Proposition 5.81(2) if
\kappa=k\geq 0
and
p_{i\kappa}=0
(since
U_{\textnormal{pers}}
is consistent) there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\hat{M}_{i}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{M}_{i}^{+})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{i}},U)
. Choose
N_{i}^{+}:=S_{\delta}
and

M_{i}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\min\{\hat{M}_{i}^{+}(t),T_{\delta}(t)\}.

Then
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N_{i}^{+},M_{i}^{+})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{i}},U)\cap Y
.

Suppose that
p_{i}
is
\kappa
-negative. Note that
U_{\textnormal{soft}}
is
\kappa
-soft (since
|{b_{i}}|
is
\kappa
-soft by Proposition 5.44(1), compare also with the proof of Subclaim 1 in Claim 1). With Corollary 5.82 we find a regular
\kappa
-soft
\hat{N}_{i}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\hat{M}_{i}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},\hat{N}_{i}^{+},\hat{M}_{i}^{+})\subset\mathcal{B}((\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{i}},U)
. The rest of the proof is similar as in the ”
\kappa
-positive”-case.

Case 2:

Let
(p_{i\kappa},...,p_{ir})=0
. Take
N_{i}^{+}:=S_{\delta}
. Note that if
|{A_{i}(t)}|<\sqrt{R}
then

|{\phi_{i}(t,z)}|=|{A_{i}(t)Q_{i}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{i,2}(t,z))|}<\sqrt{R}\cdot\sqrt[4]{R}\cdot\sqrt[4]{R}=R

for
(t,z)\in Y
by (++) and (+++) (since
T_{\delta}\leq T_{\epsilon}
). Let

M_{i}^{+}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},t\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}T_{\delta}(t),&t\in\pi(Y)\textnormal{ and }|{A_{i}(t)}|<\sqrt{R},\\
0,&\textnormal{ else.}\end{array}\right.

Note that
M_{i}^{+}
is regular and by construction that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N_{i}^{+},M_{i}^{+})\subset\mathcal{B}(\phi_{i},R)\cap Y
. We show that
M_{i}^{+}
is
\kappa
-persistent and are done.

Note that

|{A_{i}(t)Q_{i}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{i,2}(t,z))}|\leq 1

for every
(t,z)\in Y\cap C
. Let
w\in\pi_{l}(C)
. Let
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to C_{w}
be a definable curve compatible with
C_{w}
with
\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\gamma(y))\in X_{w}
and

\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))=0.

By passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary we may assume that
(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\pi(Y)
for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
(compare with the proof of Subclaim 1 in Claim 1) and that there is a definable continuous
\hat{\gamma}_{x}:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to\mathbb{R}
with
(\gamma_{u}(y),\hat{\gamma}_{x}(y))\in C_{w}\cap Y_{w}
for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. We have

|{A(w,\gamma_{u}(y))Q_{i}(w,\gamma_{u}(y),\gamma_{x}(y))e^{D_{i2}(w,\gamma_{u}(y),\gamma_{x}(y))}}|\leq 1

and therefore

\displaystyle|{A(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|\leq[Q_{i}(w,\gamma_{u}(y),\gamma_{x}(y))e^{D_{i2}(w,\gamma_{u}(y),\gamma_{x}(y))}]^{-1}\leq\sqrt{R}

for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. So we have

\lim_{y\searrow 0}M_{i}^{+}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=\lim_{y\searrow 0}T_{\delta}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0.

This gives Claim 3.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }3}

Take
N:=N^{+}
and
M:=M^{+}
where
N^{+}
and
M^{+}
are from Claim 3. From now on we consider
A_{i}
as a function on
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N(t),M(t))
for
i\in\{0,...,s\}
. We obtain the following:

If
p_{i}
is
\kappa
-negative for
i\in\{1,...,s\}
then
1/((|{A_{i}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft (since
|{b_{i}}|
is
\kappa
-soft if
p_{i}
is
\kappa
-negative by Proposition 5.44(1)) and if
g\in\log(E)
and
q
is
\kappa
-negative then
1/((|{A_{0}}|^{C})_{\textnormal{soft}})
is
\kappa
-soft (since by Proposition 5.44(2)
|{a}|
is
\kappa
-soft for
g\in\log(E)
if
q
is
\kappa
-negative) and we are done.

\blacksquare

Outgoing from this preparation result we are able to prove our desired result on integration for a holomorphic extension of a prepared restricted log-exp-analytic function in one variable.

5.85 Corollary

There is a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the following holds. Let

\Delta:=\{(t,s,z)\in\pi(C)\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\times\mathbb{C}\textnormal{ }\mid N(t)<s<M(t),z\in B(\mu_{\kappa}(t),s)\}.

Then the function

\Psi:\Delta\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,z)\mapsto\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial B(\mu_{\kappa}(t),s)}\frac{F(t,\xi)}{\xi-z}d\xi,

is well-defined. Additionally there is
l\in\mathbb{N}
and a definable function
g=(g_{1},...,g_{l}):\pi^{+}(\Delta)\to\mathbb{R}^{l}
such that
\Psi
is constructible in
z
with support function

\pi^{+}(\Delta)\to\mathbb{R}^{l}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R},(t,s)\mapsto(g(t,s),\mu_{\kappa}(t),s),

where the following holds for
g_{j}
for
j\in\{1,...,l\}
: There is
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, a regular function
\beta:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
and a log-analytic
h_{j}:\mathbb{R}^{p+1}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
g_{j}(t,s)=h_{j}(\beta(t),s)
for every
(t,s)\in\pi^{+}(\Delta)
.

Proof

By Proposition 5.84 there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\hat{M}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
F|_{\hat{W}}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with center
\Theta
with regular coefficient and base functions where
\hat{W}:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,\hat{M})
. Let

(r,\mathcal{Z},a,q,s,V,b,P)

be a complex LA-preparing tuple for
F|_{\hat{W}}
where
a
and
b
are regular.

By Corollary 5.68(2) if
\kappa=r
respectively Proposition 5.74 if
\kappa=k
we find a regular
\kappa
-persistent
\tilde{M}:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
(with
\tilde{M}<|{\mu_{r}-\mu_{k}}|
if
k\geq 0
) such that for
W:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,\tilde{M})
we have
W\subset\hat{W}
and

z_{\alpha}^{*}:W(\mu_{\kappa})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto z_{\alpha}(t,\mu_{\kappa}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
for
\alpha\in\{0,...,r\}
. (Note that

W(\mu_{\kappa})=\{(t,s)\in\pi(C)\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\textnormal{ }\mid N(t)<s<\hat{M}(t)\}\times\textnormal{}]-\pi,\pi[.)

Let
\eta:=(a,b_{1},...,b_{s})
. Note that
\eta
is regular. Similarly as in the proof of the claim in Remark 5.79 there is a globally subanalytic function
G:\mathbb{C}^{s+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{r+1}\to\mathbb{C}
such that

F(t,z)=G(\eta(t),z_{0}(t,z),...,z_{r}(t,z))

for every
(t,z)\in W
. With Remark 5.66(2) we have that

F^{*}:W(\mu_{\kappa})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto F(t,\mu_{\kappa}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is free-regular in
s
(since the components of
\eta
considered as functions on
W(\mu_{\kappa})
are free-regular in
s
). By shrinking
\tilde{M}
if necessary we may assume that
F^{*}
is bounded in
\tau
. (By Corollary 5.69 if
\kappa=r
respectively Corollary 5.75 if
\kappa=k
we obtain after shrinking
\tilde{M}
if necessary that

W(\mu_{\kappa})\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,\tau)\mapsto(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,\mu_{\kappa}(t)+(\prod_{j=0}^{\kappa}\sigma_{j})se^{i\tau}),

is bounded in
\tau
.) Choose
M:=\tilde{M}
. Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
g:=(g_{1},...,g_{l}):\pi^{+}(W(\mu_{\kappa}))\to\mathbb{R}^{l}
be a corresponding support function for
F^{*}
. With Proposition 5.63 we see that the function

\Psi:\Delta\to\mathbb{C},(t,s,z)\mapsto\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial B(\mu_{\kappa}(t),s)}\frac{F(t,\xi)}{\xi-z}d\xi,

is well-defined and constructible in
z
with support function

\pi^{+}(\Delta)\to\mathbb{R}^{l}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R},(t,s)\mapsto(g(t,s),\mu_{\kappa}(t),s).

Since
\pi^{+}(W(\mu_{\kappa}))=\pi^{+}(\Delta)
we see with Definition 5.64 that
g_{j}
fulfills the desired properties for
j\in\{1,...,l\}
.

\blacksquare

We can also strengthen Proposition 5.84 in the following way.

5.86 Proposition

There is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-soft

N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the following holds where
W:=\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa},N,M)
.

(1)

F|_{W}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared as in Proposition 5.84.

(2)

If
N(t)=0
,
M(t)>0
and
F(t,-)
has a holomorphic extension at
\mu:=\mu_{\kappa}(t)
(i.e. there is an open neighbourhood
U
of
\mu
in
B(\mu,M(t))
and a holomorphic
J:U\to\mathbb{C}
such that
J|_{U\cap W_{t}}=F(t,-)
) then there is a holomorphic function
h:B(\mu,M(t))\to\mathbb{C}
such that
h|_{W_{t}}=F(t,-)
.

Proof

By Proposition 5.84 there is a regular
\kappa
-persistent
M:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a regular
\kappa
-soft
N:\pi(C)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
F|_{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{P}_{0,\kappa}N,M)}
is complex
r
-log-analytically prepared in
z
with complex LA-preparing tuple

(r,\mathcal{Z},a,q,s,V,b,P)

such that
q_{0}=...=q_{\kappa-1}=0
,
P\in M_{\kappa}(s\times r+1,\mathbb{Q})
,
a
and
b
are regular. By shrinking
M
if necessary we can suppose

\bigl|\frac{\mathcal{P}_{j,\kappa}(t,z)}{e^{\mu_{\kappa-j-1,\kappa}(t)}}\bigl|<1/2

for
(t,z)\in D
and
j\in\{0,...,\kappa-1\}
(compare with Proposition 5.67(1)).
(+)

We have

F(t,-)=a(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,z)V(b_{1}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{1}}(t,z),...,b_{s}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{s}}(t,z)).

Since
z_{\kappa}(t,z)=\mathcal{P}_{\kappa}(t,z)
we obtain with
(+)
by successively using the logarithmic series

z_{\kappa}(t,z)=L(z-\mu)\textnormal{ }(*)

for
z\in D_{t}
where
L
is a power series around zero which converges absolutely on
B(0,M(t))
.
We set
Z:=(Z_{1},...,Z_{s})
. Let
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{s}}c_{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}
be the power series expansion of
V
.

We may assume that
a(t)\neq 0
. Note that
(p_{j\kappa},...,p_{jr})=0
or
p_{j}
is
\kappa
-positive for
j\in\{1,...,s\}
. Let

\Gamma_{1}:=\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{s}\textnormal{ }\big|\;^{t}P\alpha+q\in\{0\}^{\kappa}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\times\{0\}^{r-\kappa}\}

and
\Gamma_{2}:=\mathbb{N}_{0}^{s}\setminus\Gamma_{1}
. Set
V_{1}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{1}}c_{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}
and
V_{2}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{2}}c_{\alpha}Z^{\alpha}
. For
l\in\{1,2\}
let

G_{l}:D_{t}\to\mathbb{C},z\mapsto a(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,z)V_{l}(b_{1}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{1}}(t,z),...,b_{s}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{s}}(t,z)).

So we see by composition of power series and
(*)
that

G_{1}(z)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}d_{j}(z-\mu)^{j}

for
z\in D_{t}
where
d_{j}\in\mathbb{C}
and the series to the right converges absolutely on
B(\mu,M(t))
. So
G_{1}
has a holomorphic extension
\hat{G}_{1}
on
B(\mu,M(t))
. Let
0<\epsilon<M(t)
be such that
F|_{B(\mu,\epsilon)\cap D_{t}}
has a holomorphic extension
\hat{F}
on
W:=B(\mu,\epsilon)
. Set

\hat{G}_{2}:W\to\mathbb{C},z\mapsto\hat{F}(z)-\hat{G}_{1}(z).

Note that
\hat{G}_{2}
is holomorphic and that
\hat{G}_{2}|_{D_{t}\cap W}=G_{2}
. We show that
\hat{G}_{2}=0
and we are done by the identity theorem (since
F_{t}
coincides then with
G_{1}
on
W\cap D_{t}
). Let

\Omega:=\{(\omega_{\kappa},...,\omega_{r})\in\mathbb{Q}^{r-\kappa+1}\mid\textnormal{ there is }\alpha\in\Gamma_{2}\textnormal{ such that }

(^{t}P\alpha+q)_{j}=\omega_{j}\textnormal{ for }j\in\{\kappa,...,r\}\}.

Then
\Omega\subset\mathbb{Q}^{r-\kappa+1}\setminus(\mathbb{N}_{0}\times\{0\}^{r-\kappa})
. For
\omega\in\Omega
let

\Gamma_{2,\omega}:=\{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{s}\mid(^{t}P\alpha+q)_{j}=\omega_{j}\textnormal{ for }j\in\{\kappa,...,r\}\}.

We have

\hat{G}_{2}(z)=\sum_{\omega\in\Omega}e_{\omega}\prod_{j=\kappa}^{r}(\sigma_{j}z_{j}(t,z))^{\omega_{j}}

where

e_{\omega}=a(t)\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma_{2,\omega}}c_{\alpha}\prod_{j=1}^{s}b_{j}(t)^{\alpha_{j}}

for
z\in W
. Let

\Omega^{*}:=\{\omega\in\Omega\mid e_{\omega}\neq 0\}.

If
\Omega^{*}=\emptyset
we are done. So assume
\Omega^{*}\neq\emptyset
.
There is
\xi:=(\xi_{\kappa},...,\xi_{r})\in\Omega
such that
\prod_{j=\kappa}^{r}|{z_{j}}|^{\omega_{j}}=o(\prod_{j=\kappa}^{r}|{z_{j}}|^{\xi_{j}})

for all
\omega\in\Omega
with
\omega\neq\xi
. By investigating the following two cases we are done.

Case 1:

If
\xi_{\kappa}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
(note that
\xi\neq 0
) we differentiate
\hat{G}_{2}

m
-times with respect to
z
where
m:=\xi_{\kappa}+1
and we see with Corollary 5.35 (note that
\xi\neq 0
) that there is
\beta=(-1,\beta_{\kappa+1},...,\beta_{r})\in\mathbb{Q}^{r-\kappa+1}
such that

\lim_{z\to\mu}\Bigl|\frac{d^{m}\hat{G}_{2}/dz^{m}(z)}{\prod_{j=\kappa}^{r}z_{j}^{\beta_{j}}}\Bigl|\in\mathbb{R}^{*}.

But we obtain

\lim_{z\to\mu}\frac{d^{m}\hat{G}_{2}}{dz^{m}}(z)=\frac{d^{m}\hat{G}_{2}}{dz^{m}}(\mu)\in\mathbb{C},

a contradiction.

Case 2:

If
\xi_{\kappa}\notin\mathbb{N}_{0}
(note that
\xi\neq 0
) we differentiate
\hat{G}_{2}

m
-times with respect to
z
where
m:=\lceil{\xi_{\kappa}}\rceil
and we see with Corollary 5.35 that there is
\gamma=(\gamma_{\kappa},...,\gamma_{r})\in\mathbb{Q}^{r-k+1}
with
\gamma_{\kappa}<0
such that

\lim_{z\to\mu}\Bigl|\frac{d^{m}\hat{G}_{2}/dz^{m}(z)}{\prod_{j=\kappa}^{r}z_{j}^{\gamma_{j}}}\Bigl|\in\mathbb{R}^{*}.

We get the same contradiction as in Case 1.

\blacksquare

We close Section 5.1 by giving two more technical results which we need to show that a real analytic restricted log-exp-analytic function has a unary high parametric global complexification which is again restricted log-exp-analytic. For the rest of this section we set the following.

•

If
\prod_{j=0}^{r}\sigma_{j}=1
and
k<0
we set

H_{\textnormal{small}}:=\{(t,z)\in\pi(C)\times\mathbb{C}\mid\textnormal{Re}(z)\in\textnormal{}]\mu_{r}(t),\infty[\}.

•

If
\prod_{j=0}^{r}\sigma_{j}=-1
and
k<0
we set

H_{\textnormal{small}}:=\{(t,z)\in\pi(C)\times\mathbb{C}\mid\textnormal{Re}(z)\in\textnormal{}]-\infty,\mu_{r}(t)[\}.

•

If
\prod_{j=0}^{r}\sigma_{j}=1
and
k\geq 0
we set

H_{\textnormal{small}}:=\{(t,z)\in\pi(C)\times\mathbb{C}\mid\textnormal{Re}(z)\in\textnormal{}]\mu_{r}(t),\mu_{k}(t)[\}.

•

If
\prod_{j=0}^{r}\sigma_{j}=-1
and
k\geq 0
we set

H_{\textnormal{small}}:=\{(t,z)\in\pi(C)\times\mathbb{C}\mid\textnormal{Re}(z)\in\textnormal{}]\mu_{k}(t),\mu_{r}(t)[\}.

Note that
H_{\textnormal{small}}
is definable and that
C\subset H_{\textnormal{small}}\subset H
(compare with Remark 5.13).

5.87 Proposition

Let
g\in\log(E)
. Let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Let
\gamma,\hat{\gamma}:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to(\Lambda_{g})_{w}\cap(H_{\textnormal{small}})_{w}
be definable curves compatible with
C_{w}
where
\hat{\gamma}
runs also through
\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\gamma_{u}=\hat{\gamma}_{u}
. Suppose that

•

\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\gamma_{z}(y)-\hat{\gamma}_{z}(y))=0
,

•

\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\gamma_{z}(y)-\mu_{\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|>0
for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k,r\}
if
k\geq 0
, and

•

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma(y)\in X_{w}
.

Then we have

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\Phi_{g}(w,\gamma(y))=\lim_{y\searrow 0}\Phi_{g}(w,\hat{\gamma}(y))\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof

Note that
g
is log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared in
x
with respect to
E
for
l\in\{-1,...,e-1\}
. We do an induction on
l
. For
l=-1
the statement is clear.

l-1\to l:
Let

(r,\mathcal{Y},a,e^{d_{0}},q,s,v,b,e^{d},P):=(r,\mathcal{Y},a_{g},e^{d_{0,g}},q_{g},s,v_{g},b_{g},e^{d_{g}},P_{g})

where
b_{g}:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
,
\mathrm{exp}(d_{g}):=(\mathrm{exp}(d_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(d_{s}))
and
P_{g}:=(p_{1},...,p_{s})^{t}
.
Note that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}b_{i}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\mathbb{R}
for
i\in\{1,...,s\}
by Remark 5.53(1) and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}a(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\mathbb{R}
by Remark 5.53(2) (since
g
is locally bounded in
(u,x)
with reference set
X
). Let

\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{C}}:=(r,\mathcal{Z},a,\mathrm{exp}(D_{0}),q,s,V,b,\mathrm{exp}(D),P)

be a complex preparing tuple for
\Phi_{g}
where
D_{j}:=\Phi_{d_{j}}
for
j\in\{0,...,s\}
and
\mathrm{exp}(D):=(\mathrm{exp}(D_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(D_{s}))
. By the inductive hypothesis we have

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\mathrm{exp}(D_{j}(w,\gamma(y)))=\lim_{y\searrow 0}\mathrm{exp}(D_{j}(w,\hat{\gamma}(y)))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

for
j\in\{0,...,s\}
.

Claim

Let
\alpha\in\{0,...,r\}
. Then we have

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{\alpha}z_{\alpha}(w,\gamma(y))=\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{\alpha}z_{\alpha}(w,\hat{\gamma}(y))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

Proof of the claim

Note that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\hat{\gamma}_{x}(y)-\mu_{j}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|>0
for
j\in\{0,...,\alpha\}
. Since
\sigma_{\alpha}z_{\alpha}(w,\hat{\gamma}_{u}(y))>0
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
we obtain

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{\alpha}z_{\alpha}(w,\hat{\gamma}_{u}(y))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

with
(II_{\alpha})
in Proposition 5.28.

We show the statement by induction on
\alpha
. For
\alpha=0
we have with
(I_{0})
in Proposition 5.28

\displaystyle\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{0}z_{0}(w,\gamma(y))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{0}(\gamma_{z}(y)-\Theta_{0}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{0}(\hat{\gamma}_{z}(y)-\Theta_{0}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

\alpha-1\to\alpha
: We have with the inductive hypothesis,
(I_{\alpha})
in Proposition 5.28 and the continuity of the global logarithm

\displaystyle\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{\alpha}z_{\alpha}(w,\gamma(y))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\sigma_{\alpha}(\log(\sigma_{\alpha-1}z_{\alpha-1}(w,\gamma(y)))-\Theta_{\alpha}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\sigma_{\alpha}(\log(\sigma_{\alpha-1}z_{\alpha-1}(w,\hat{\gamma}(y)))-\Theta_{\alpha}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{y\searrow 0}\sigma_{\alpha}z_{\alpha}(w,\hat{\gamma}(y))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim}}

By the claim we have

\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\nu}(\gamma(y))=\lim_{y\searrow 0}(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes\nu}(\hat{\gamma}(y))\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}

for every
\nu\in\{q,p_{1},...,p_{s}\}
and we are done since
V
is a continuous function on
\overline{D^{s}(0,R)}
.
\blacksquare

For a definable
Z\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{C}
and
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
we set

\zeta_{Z}(t,x):=\sup\{y\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\mid x+iQ(0,y)\subset Z_{t}\}.

5.88 Proposition

Let
g\in\log(E)\cup\{f\}
. Let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Let
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to(H_{\textnormal{small}})_{w}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R})
be a definable curve compatible with
C_{w}
with

•

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma(y)\in X_{w}
,

•

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{dist}(\gamma_{x}(y),C_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))})=0
,

•

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0

for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k,r\}
if
k\geq 0
.

Then we have
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Lambda_{g}}(w,\gamma(y))>0
.

Proof

Let
(u_{0},x_{0}):=\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma(y)
. Assume that
g
is log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared in
x
with respect to
E
for
l\in\{-1,...,e\}
. We do an induction on
l
. The assertion is clear for
l=-1
(since
\Lambda_{g}=\pi(C)\times\mathbb{C}
).

l-1\to l:

Let

(r,\mathcal{Y},a,e^{d_{0}},q,s,v,b,e^{d},P):=(r,\mathcal{Y},a_{g},e^{d_{0,g}},q_{g},s,v_{g},b_{g},e^{d_{g}},P_{g})

where
b_{g}:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
,
\mathrm{exp}(d_{g}):=(\mathrm{exp}(d_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(d_{s}))
and
P:=(p_{1},...,p_{s})^{t}
. Let

\mathcal{J}_{\mathbb{C}}=(r,\mathcal{Z},a,\mathrm{exp}(D_{0}),q,s,V,b,\mathrm{exp}(D),P)

be a complex preparing tuple for
\Phi_{g}
where
D_{j}:=\Phi_{d_{j}}
for
j\in\{0,...,s\}
and
\mathrm{exp}(D):=(\mathrm{exp}(D_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(D_{s}))
. We set for
j\in\{1,...,s\}
and
(t,z)\in\Lambda_{g}

\phi_{j}(t,z):=b_{j}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{j}}(t,z)e^{D_{j}(t,z)}.

Note that
|{\phi_{j}(t,z)}|\leq 1
if
(t,z)\in C
. By the inductive hypothesis and passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary we may assume that
(w,\gamma(y))\in\Lambda_{d_{i}}
for
i\in\{0,...,s\}
and every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
.

Assume
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Lambda_{g}}(w,\gamma(y))=0
. Then there is a definable curve
\hat{\gamma}:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to(H_{\textnormal{small}})_{w}
with
\hat{\gamma}_{u}=\gamma_{u}
,
\textnormal{Re}(\hat{\gamma})=\gamma_{x}
,
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\hat{\gamma}_{z}(y)=x_{0}
and
0<\epsilon<1
such that
\hat{\gamma}_{z}(y)\notin(\Lambda_{g})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}
for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[
.

By shrinking
\epsilon
if necessary we may assume that
\hat{\gamma}(y)\in(\Lambda_{d_{i}})_{w}
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[
and
i\in\{0,...,s\}
. By further shrinking
\epsilon
if necessary we find
j^{*}\in\{0,...,s\}
such that

|{\textnormal{Im}(D_{j^{*}}(w,\hat{\gamma}(y)))}|\geq\pi

for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[
or
j^{+}\in\{1,...,s\}
such that
|{\phi_{j^{+}}(w,\hat{\gamma}(y))}|\geq R
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[
. By Remark 5.27(2) there is a definable curve
\tilde{\gamma}:\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[\textnormal{}\to C_{w}
compatible with
C_{w}
with
\tilde{\gamma}_{u}=\hat{\gamma}_{u}=\gamma_{u}
and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\tilde{\gamma}(y)=(u_{0},x_{0})
. Since
\tilde{\gamma}
runs through
C_{w}
we have
|{\phi_{j^{+}}(w,\tilde{\gamma}(y))}|\leq 1
for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[
and therefore
\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\phi_{j^{+}}(w,\tilde{\gamma}(y))}|\leq 1
. So the latter cannot hold by Proposition 5.87 (since
R>1
). Since
\tilde{\gamma}
runs through
C_{w}
we obtain

\textnormal{Im}(D_{j^{*}}(w,\tilde{\gamma}(y)))=\textnormal{Im}(d_{j^{*}}(w,\tilde{\gamma}(y)))=0

for
y\textnormal{}\in\textnormal{}]0,\epsilon[
. So the former cannot hold by Proposition 5.87, a contradiction.

\blacksquare

5.2
Global Complexification of Restricted

Log-Exp-Analytic Functions

Now we are able to show that a real analytic restricted log-exp-analytic function has a global complexification which is again restricted log-exp-analytic. The idea is to show that a real analytic restricted log-exp-analytic function has a unary high parametric global complexification which is again restricted log-exp-analytic and then to do a technical induction on the number of variables. We start with the following result from analysis.

5.89 Proposition

Let
Y
be a non-empty topological space. Let
g:Y\to\mathbb{R}
be a function that is bounded from below. Let

f:Y\to\mathbb{R},\textnormal{ }x\mapsto\liminf\limits_{y\to x}g(y).

Then
f
is lower semi-continuous, i.e. for every
x\in Y
and every
a<f(x)
there is an open neighbourhood
V
of
x
such that
a<f(y)
for all
y\in V
.

Proof

See for example Bourbaki (
[
4
]
, Chapter IV, §6, Section 2, Proposition 4).
\blacksquare

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
, definable sets
X,Y\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
with
Y\subset X
, a function
f:Y\to\mathbb{R}
and a set
E
of positive definable functions on
X
we say that
f
can be constructed from
E
if
f
can be constructed from
E|_{Y}
.

5.2.1
The One Dimensional Case

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
t
range over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
x
over
\mathbb{R}
.
Let
l,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be with

. Let
w
range over
\mathbb{R}^{l}
and
u
over
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. For a definable set
Z\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{C}
and
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
we set

\zeta_{Z}:=\sup\{r\geq 0\mid x+iQ(0,r)\subset Z_{t}\}.

5.90 Proposition

Let
X\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be definable such that
X_{w}
is open for every

. Let

.

For a definable cell

C
-consistent” means always ”
C
-consistent in

with respect to

” and ”

”.

of order at most

. By Proposition 4.12 there is

and a definable cell decomposition

of

For every

. For

where

Step 1: Constructing a suitable holomorphic extension of

in the last variable

for

Suppose that

\emptyset,&t\notin\pi(C).\end{array}\right.

We define
G_{C}:\hat{\Xi}_{C}\to\mathbb{C}
by letting

(G_{C})_{t}(z):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\Phi_{C})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\Gamma_{C})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{t}\textnormal{ and }\\
(\chi_{C,r})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\mathcal{G}_{C,r})_{t},&t\in\hat{B}_{C,r},\\
(\Phi_{C})_{t}(z),&t\in\pi(C)\setminus\hat{B}_{C,r}.\end{array}\right.

Note that
(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}
is open for every
t\in\pi(C)
and that
G_{C}
is a well-defined unary parametric global complexification of
f|_{C}:C\to\mathbb{R}
.

Suppose that
k:=k_{C}\geq 0
. Let for
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\Gamma_{C})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,k})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{t},&t\in\hat{B}_{C,k}\cap\hat{B}_{C,r},\\
(\Gamma_{C})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,k})_{t},&t\in\hat{B}_{C,k}\setminus\hat{B}_{C,r},\\
(\Gamma_{C})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{t},&t\in\hat{B}_{C,r}\setminus\hat{B}_{C,k},\\
(\Gamma_{C})_{t},&t\in\pi(C)\setminus(\hat{B}_{C,k}\cup\hat{B}_{C,r}),\\
\emptyset,&t\notin\pi(C).\end{array}\right.

Finally define
G_{C}:\hat{\Xi}_{C}\to\mathbb{C}
by letting

(G_{C})_{t}(z):=

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\Phi_{C})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\Gamma_{C})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,k})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{t}\textnormal{ and }(\chi_{C,r})_{t}(z)\\
\textnormal{ if }z\in(\mathcal{G}_{C,r})_{t}\textnormal{ and }(\chi_{C,k})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\mathcal{G}_{C,k})_{t},&t\in\hat{B}_{C,k}\cap\hat{B}_{C,r},\\
(\Phi_{C})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\Gamma_{C})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,k})_{t}\textnormal{ and }(\chi_{C,k})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\mathcal{G}_{C,k})_{t},&t\in\hat{B}_{C,k}\setminus\hat{B}_{C,r},\\
(\Phi_{C})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\Gamma_{C})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{t}\textnormal{ and }(\chi_{C,r})_{t}(z)\textnormal{ if }z\in(\mathcal{G}_{C,r})_{t},&t\in\hat{B}_{C,r}\setminus\hat{B}_{C,k},\\
(\Phi_{C})_{t}(z),&\textnormal{ else.}\end{array}\right.

Note that
(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}
is open for every
t\in\pi(C)
and that
G_{C}
is a well-defined unary parametric global complexification of
f|_{C}:C\to\mathbb{R}
.

Claim 1

Let
C\in\mathcal{C}
. Let
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k_{C},r\}
if
k_{C}\geq 0
. Let
t\in\pi(C)
be with
N_{C,\kappa}(t)=0
and
0<M_{C,\kappa}(t)
. Suppose there is
\epsilon>0
such that
Q(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\subset X_{t}
and
f_{t}(x)=p(x-\mu_{C,\kappa}(t))
for
x\in Q(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)
where
p
is a convergent power series on
]-\epsilon,\epsilon[
. Suppose that
C_{t}\cap B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\neq\emptyset
. Then
t\in\hat{B}_{C,\kappa}
and therefore
B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),M_{C,\kappa}(t))\cap(H_{C})_{t}\subset(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}.

Proof of Claim 1

By (+) and the identity theorem we see that
(\Phi_{C}|_{K_{C}})_{t}
has a holomorphic extension
h
on
B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\hat{M}_{C,\kappa}(t))
. Since
\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t)=\hat{M}_{C,\kappa}(t)/2
we get by Cauchy’s integral formula that

h(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t))}\frac{(\Phi_{C})_{t}(\xi)}{\xi-z}d\xi

for all
z\in B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t))
. So we get that

B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t))\to\mathbb{C},z\mapsto\Psi_{C,\kappa}(t,\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t),z),

coincides with
h
and is therefore holomorphic. Hence
t\in\hat{B}_{C,\kappa}
. This gives

B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),M_{C,\kappa}(t))\cap(H_{C})_{t}\subset(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }1}

Claim 2

Let
C\in\mathcal{C}
. Let
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k_{C},r\}
if
k_{C}\geq 0
. Let
t\in\pi(C)
be with
N_{C,\kappa}(t)\neq 0
. Suppose there is
\epsilon>0
such that
Q(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\subset X_{t}
and
f_{t}(x)=p(x-\mu_{C,\kappa}(t))
for
x\in Q(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)
where
p
is a convergent power series on
]-\epsilon,\epsilon[
. Suppose that
\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t)<\min\{\hat{M}_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon\}
and that
C_{t}\cap B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\neq\emptyset
. Then
t\in\hat{B}_{C,\kappa}
and therefore
B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),M_{C,\kappa}(t))\cap(H_{C})_{t}\subset(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}
.

Proof of Claim 2

Note that
\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t)=2N_{C,\kappa}(t)
. By power series expansion there is a holomorphic extension
h:B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\to\mathbb{C}
of
f_{t}:Q(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\textnormal{}\to\mathbb{R}
.
We have
h=(\Phi_{C})_{t}
on
B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\cap(\tilde{\Gamma}_{C})_{t}
by the identity theorem (since
B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon)\cap(\tilde{\Gamma}_{C})_{t}
is connected).
By Cauchy’s integral formula we get for all
z\in B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t))
that

h(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t))}\frac{(\Phi_{C})_{t}(\xi)}{\xi-z}d\xi

since
N_{C,\kappa}(t)<\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t)<\min\{\hat{M}_{C,\kappa}(t),\epsilon\}
. So we get that

B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t))\to\mathbb{C},z\mapsto\Psi_{C,\kappa}(t,\alpha_{C,\kappa}(t),z),

coincides with
h
and is therefore holomorphic. Hence
t\in\hat{B}_{C,\kappa}
. We obtain

B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(t),M_{C,\kappa}(t))\cap(H_{C})_{t}\subset(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }2}

Step 2: Constructing a unary high parametric global complexification
G:Z\to\mathbb{R}
of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
.

Now we are ready to construct a unary high parametric global complexification
G:Z\to\mathbb{C}
of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
by gluing the single functions
G_{C}
together. In step 3 below we show that
G
can be ”redefined” to a function
F
which is indeed restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,z)
(i.e. there is a restricted log-exp-analytic function
F:Z\to\mathbb{R}
in
(u,z)
which coincides with
G
).

We say that
(t,z)\in\hat{\Xi}_{C}
fulfills property
(\omega)
if the following holds.

(\omega_{1})

(t,\textnormal{Re}(z))\in X
.

(\omega_{2})

There is an open neighbourhood
U
of
z
in
(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}
such that for every
v_{1}+iv_{2}\in U
we have that
v_{1}+i]0,v_{2}[\textnormal{ }\subset(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}
if
\textnormal{Re}(v_{2})>0
or
v_{1}+i]v_{2},0[\textnormal{ }\subset(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}
if
\textnormal{Re}(v_{2})<0
.

(\omega_{3})

The point
z
is in the same connected component of
(\hat{\Xi}_{C})_{t}
as
C_{t}
.

Let

\Xi_{C}:=\{(t,z)\in\hat{\Xi}_{C}\mid(t,z)\textnormal{ fulfills property }(\omega)\}.

Then
\Xi_{C}
is definable,
C_{t}\subset(\Xi_{C})_{t}
and
(\Xi_{C})_{t}
is open for
t\in\pi(C)
. Let

\Omega:=X\cup\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}}\Xi_{C}.

Then
\Omega
is definable and it is easy to check with Proposition 5.88 and Claim 1 that
\Omega_{t}
is open for every
t\in\pi(C)
. We define

G:\Omega\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}G_{C}(t,z),&(t,z)\in\Xi_{C},\\
f(t,z),&(t,z)\in X.\end{array}\right.

By definition of
\Omega
and the identity theorem we see that
G:\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
is a well-defined unary parametric global complexification of
f:X\to\mathbb{R}
.

We show that
G
is a unary high parametric global complexification of
f:X\to\mathbb{R}
with respect to
(u,x)
. This is done with the following

Claim 3

Let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
and
(u_{0},x_{0})\in X_{w}
. Then

\liminf_{(s,v)\to(u_{0},x_{0})}\zeta_{\Omega}(w,s,v)>0.

Proof of Claim 3

We find
\epsilon>0
such that
W^{*}:=Q^{m}(u_{0},\epsilon)\textnormal{}\times]x_{0}-\epsilon,x_{0}+\epsilon[\textnormal{}\subset X_{w}
and
f_{w}(u,x)=p(u-u_{0},x-x_{0})
for
(u,x)\in W^{*}
for a convergent power series
p
on
Q^{m+1}(0,\epsilon)
. We assume

\liminf_{(s,v)\to(u_{0},x_{0})}\zeta_{\Omega}(w,s,v)=0.

Then there is a definable curve
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to Q^{m}(u_{0},\epsilon)\times]x_{0}-\epsilon/2,x_{0}+\epsilon/2[,y\mapsto(\gamma_{u}(y),\gamma_{x}(y)),
with
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma(y)=(u_{0},x_{0})
such that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Omega}(w,\gamma(y))=0
. For
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
let

I(y):=\{C\in\mathcal{C}\mid C_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\cap\textrm{}]x_{0}-\epsilon/2,x_{0}+\epsilon/2[\textrm{}\neq\emptyset\}.

By passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary we may assume that
I(y)
is independent of
y
. We set

I_{\textrm{big}}:=\{C\in I:\lim_{y\searrow 0}L_{C}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0\}

and

I_{\textrm{small}}:=\{C\in I:\lim_{y\searrow 0}L_{C}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=0\}.

Because
\sum_{C\in I}L_{C}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\geq\epsilon
for all
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
we see that
I_{\textrm{big}}\neq\emptyset
. For
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
we choose
C_{y}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
(w,\gamma(y))\in\overline{C_{y}}
. We may assume that
C^{*}:=C_{y}
is independent of
y
by passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary. We also have
(u_{0},x_{0})\in\overline{(C^{*})_{w}}
. Let
k^{*}:=k_{C^{*}}
. We consider two cases.

Case 1:

C^{*}\in I_{\textrm{big}}
. Note that
\gamma
is compatible with
(C^{*})_{w}
.

Subcase 1.1:

Assume
\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|>0
for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k^{*},r\}
if
k^{*}\geq 0
. By passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary we may assume that
\gamma(y)\in(H_{C^{*},\textnormal{small}})_{w}
for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. So by Proposition 5.88 we obtain

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Lambda_{C^{*}}}(w,\gamma(y))>0.

Note also that

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\mathrm{exp}(-|{\log(e_{C^{*},j}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))}|)>0

for
j\in\{1,...,l_{C^{*}}^{*}\}
since
(u_{0},x_{0})\in X_{w}
,
e_{C^{*},j}\in\mathcal{E}_{C^{*}}
(and so
e_{C^{*},j}
is
C^{*}
-consistent) and
\gamma
is compatible with
(C^{*})_{w}
. Since
C^{*}\in I_{\textnormal{big}}
and
\Gamma_{C^{*}}=\hat{\Gamma}_{C^{*}}\cap H_{C^{*},\textnormal{small}}
we obtain

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Gamma_{C^{*}}}(w,\gamma(y))>0.

Therefore

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Omega}(w,\gamma(y))\geq\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Gamma_{C^{*}}}(w,\gamma(y))>0

by construction of
\Omega
, a contradiction.

Subcase 1.2:

Suppose that

\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|=0

for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k^{*},r\}
if
k^{*}\geq 0
. Fix this
\kappa
. Since
M_{C^{*},\kappa}
is
(C^{*},\kappa)
-persistent respectively
N_{C^{*},\kappa}
is
(C^{*},\kappa)
-soft we have with Definition 5.38 that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}M_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0
and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}N_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=0
(by considering a definable curve
\hat{\gamma}
in
C^{*}_{w}
converging to
(u_{0},x_{0})
with
\hat{\gamma}_{u}=\gamma_{u}
. Such a definable curve is compatible with
C_{w}^{*}
and exists by Remark 5.27(2) since
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{dist}(\gamma_{x}(y),(C^{*})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))})=0
). For
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
set

\mathcal{S}(y):=\min\{M_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),\epsilon/2\}.

By passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary we may assume that

|{\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|<1/2\mathcal{S}(y),\textnormal{ }(*)

that

B(\mu_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),1/2\mathcal{S}(y))\cap C^{*}_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\neq\emptyset\textnormal{ }(**)

and that
2N_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))<1/2\mathcal{S}(y)
for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. Additionally we may suppose that either
N_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0
for all
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
or
N_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=0
for all
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
.

Assume the former. Then we have
\alpha_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=2N_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))
and therefore
\alpha_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))<1/2\mathcal{S}(y)
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. By Claim 2 and
(**)
we have

B(\mu_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),M_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))\cap(H_{C^{*}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\subset(\hat{\Xi}_{C^{*}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}

for all
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. We have with the construction of
\Xi_{C^{*}}
and
M_{C^{*},\kappa}

B(\mu_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),\mathcal{S}(y))\cap(H_{C^{*}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\subset(\Xi_{C^{*}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}

and therefore

B(\mu_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),\mathcal{S}(y))\subset\Omega_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}

for all
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. This gives with
(*)
and Pythagoras

\{\gamma_{x}(y)+iv\in\mathbb{C}\mid v\in\textnormal{}]0,\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\mathcal{S}(y)[\}\subset\Omega_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}

for all
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. So we have

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Omega}(w,\gamma(y))\geq\lim\limits_{y\searrow 0}\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\mathcal{S}(y)=\lim\limits_{y\searrow 0}\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\min\{M_{C^{*},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),\epsilon/2\}>0,

since
M_{C^{*},\kappa}
is
(C^{*},\kappa)
-persistent, a contradiction. The latter case is handled completely similar.

Case 2:

C^{*}\in I_{\textrm{small}}
.

Note that there is
C_{\mathcal{L}}\in I_{\textnormal{big}}
with
C_{\mathcal{L}}<C^{*}
such that

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{dist}(\gamma_{x}(y),(C_{\mathcal{L}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))})=0.

So
(u_{0},x_{0})\in\overline{(C_{\mathcal{L}})_{w}}
.

Subcase 2.1:

Suppose that

\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{C_{\mathcal{L}},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|>0

for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k_{C_{\mathcal{L}}},r\}
if
k_{C_{\mathcal{L}}}\geq 0
. By passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary we may assume that
\gamma
runs through
(H_{C_{\mathcal{L}},\textnormal{small}})_{w}
since for
\mu_{C_{\mathcal{L}},\kappa}\geq C_{\mathcal{L}}

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{dist}(\mu_{C_{\mathcal{L}},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),(C_{\mathcal{L}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))})>0

for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k_{C_{\mathcal{L}}},r\}
if
k_{C_{\mathcal{L}}}\geq 0
. Note that
\gamma
is compatible with
(C_{\mathcal{L}})_{w}
. By Proposition 5.88 we obtain

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{\Lambda_{C_{\mathcal{L}}}}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0.

A completely similar argument as in Subcase 1.1 applied to
C_{\mathcal{L}}
gives the desired contradiction.

Subcase 2.2:

Suppose

\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\gamma_{x}(y)-\mu_{C_{\mathcal{L}},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|=0

for
\kappa=r
or a
\kappa\in\{k_{C_{\mathcal{L}}},r\}
if
k_{C_{\mathcal{L}}}\geq 0
. Fix such a
\kappa
. Then

\lim_{y\searrow 0}N_{C_{\mathcal{L}},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=0

and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}M_{C_{\mathcal{L}},\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0
by Definition 5.38 (by considering a suitable definable curve
\hat{\gamma}
in
(C_{\mathcal{L}})_{w}
converging to
(u_{0},x_{0})
with
\hat{\gamma}_{u}=\gamma_{u}
. Such a curve is compatible with
(C_{\mathcal{L}})_{w}
and exists by Remark 5.27(2) since

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{dist}(\gamma_{x}(y),(C_{\mathcal{L}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))})=0.)

A completely similar argument as in subcase 1.2 applied to
C_{\mathcal{L}}
gives the desired contradiction.
\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }3}

We set

Z:=\{(w,u,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{l}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{C}\mid(w,u,\textnormal{Re}(z))\in X\textnormal{ and }

\textnormal{Im}(z)\in Q(0,\rho_{\Omega}(w,u,\textnormal{Re}(z)))\}

where for
(w,u,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{l}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}

\rho_{\Omega}(w,u,x):=\liminf_{(s,v)\to(u,x)}\zeta_{\Omega}(w,s,v).

Note that
Z
is definable with
X\subset Z
by Claim 3.

Claim 4

Z_{w}
is open for every
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
(considered as a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
).

Proof of Claim 4

Let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
and
(u_{0},z_{0})\in Z_{w}
. Let
z_{0}:=x_{0}+iy_{0}
for
x_{0},y_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then there is
r>0
such that
y_{0}<r<\rho_{\Omega}(w,u_{0},x_{0})
. Since the function

\rho_{\Omega}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R},(u,x)\mapsto\liminf_{(s,v)\to(u,x)}\zeta_{\Omega}(w,s,v),

is lower semi-continuous by Proposition 5.89 we find an open neighbourhood
U
of
(u_{0},x_{0})
contained in the open set
X_{w}
such that
\rho_{\Omega}(w,u,x)>r
for every
(u,x)\in U
. This shows that
V:=U+iQ(0,r)\subset Z_{w}
and we have
(u_{0},z_{0})\in V
.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }4}

Claim 4 shows that
G|_{Z}
is a unary high parametric global complexification of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
.

Step 3: Constructing a unary high parametric global complexification
F:Z\to\mathbb{R}
of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
which is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,z)
.

Now we are able to redefine
G
suitably in order to obtain the desired function
F
.

Let
\mathcal{B}:=\{\pi(C)\mid C\in\mathcal{C}\}
. Then
\mathcal{B}
is a definable cell decomposition of
\pi(X)
. For
B\in\mathcal{B}
consider the following.

Set

\mathcal{C}_{B}:=\{C\in\mathcal{C}\mid\pi(C)=B\}

and let
X_{B}:=\{(t,x)\in X\mid t\in B\}
and
Z_{B}:=\{(t,z)\in Z\mid t\in B\}
. Then
X=\dot{\bigcup}_{B\in\mathcal{B}}X_{B}
and
Z=\dot{\bigcup}_{B\in\mathcal{B}}Z_{B}
. Let
u_{B}
be the cardinality of
\mathcal{C}_{B}
. Let
C_{B,1},...,C_{B,u_{B}}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}
be the unique cells with
C_{B,1}<...<C_{B,u_{B}}
. Note that
C_{B,j}
is fat with respect to
x
for
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
. For
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
we set
\Gamma_{B,j}:=\Gamma_{C_{B,j}}
,
\Xi_{B,j}:=\Xi_{C_{B,j}}
,
\mathcal{E}_{B,j}:=\mathcal{E}_{C_{B,j}}
,
\Phi_{B,j}:=\Phi_{C_{B,j}}
,
k_{B,j}:=k_{C_{B,j}}
,
G_{B,j}:=G_{C_{B,j}}
and for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k_{B,j},r\}
if
k_{B,j}\geq 0
we set
\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa}:=\mathcal{T}_{C_{B,j},\kappa}
. From now on we consider every
g\in\mathcal{E}_{B,j}
as a function on
\Xi_{B,j}
.

Let
Y_{B,j}:=(X_{B}\cup\Xi_{B,j})\cap Z
for
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
. We define

Z_{B,1}:=\{(t,z)\in Y_{B,1}\mid\zeta_{Y_{B,1}}(t,\textnormal{Re}(z))\geq\zeta_{Y_{B,j}}(t,\textnormal{Re}(z))

\textnormal{ for every }j\in\{2,...,u_{B}\}\}

and inductively for
l\in\{2,...,u_{B}\}

Z_{B,l}:=\{(t,z)\in Y_{B,l}\mid\zeta_{Y_{B,l}}(t,\textnormal{Re}(z))\geq\zeta_{Y_{B,j}}(t,\textnormal{Re}(z))\textnormal{ for every }

j\in\{l+1,...,u_{B}\}\textnormal{ and }(t,z)\notin Z_{B,1}\cup...\cup Z_{B,l-1}\}.

Note that
Z_{B,j}
is definable for
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
and that
Z_{B}=\dot{\bigcup}_{j=1}^{u_{B}}Z_{B,j}
. Note also that for
(t,x)\in X_{B}
there is a unique
j\in\{1,....,l\}
such that
(t,x+iy)\in Z_{B,j}
for every
y\in\mathbb{R}
with
(t,x+iy)\in Z_{B}
. For
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
set

X_{B,j}:=\{(t,x)\in X_{B}\mid(t,x)\in Z_{B,j}\}.

Note that
X_{B}=\dot{\bigcup}_{j=1}^{u_{B}}X_{B,j}
. Fix
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
. We have

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{Z_{B,j}}(w,\gamma_{u}(y),\gamma_{x}(y))>0

for
(w,u_{0},x_{0})\in X
and a definable curve
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to(X_{B,j})_{w}
converging to
(u_{0},x_{0})
.
(++)

(Otherwise

\liminf_{(u,x)\to(u_{0},x_{0})}\zeta_{Z}(w,u,x)=0

by construction of
Z_{B,j}
which is a contradiction to the fact that
F
is a unary high parametric global complexification of
f
which respect to
(u,x)
.)

If
k_{B,j}<0
we set
Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}:=\mathcal{T}_{B,j,r}\cap Z_{B,j}
. If
k_{B,j}\geq 0
we set
Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}:=(\mathcal{T}_{B,j,k_{B,j}}\dot{\cup}\textnormal{ }\mathcal{T}_{B,j,r})\cap Z_{B,j}
. Set
Z_{B,j,\Gamma}:=(\Gamma_{B,j}\cap Z_{B,j})\setminus Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
. Then
Z_{B,j}=Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}\ \dot{\cup}\ Z_{B,j,\Gamma}
.

For
B\in\mathcal{B}
and
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
let

E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}:=\mathcal{E}_{B,j}|_{Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}\setminus X}

and

E_{B,j,\Gamma}:=(\mathcal{E}_{B,j}\cup E_{\Phi_{B,j}})|_{Z_{B,j,\Gamma}\setminus X}.

Additionally consider

F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}:Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}\setminus X\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto G_{B,j}(t,z),

and

F_{B,j,\Gamma}:Z_{B,j,\Gamma}\setminus X\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto\Phi_{B,j}(t,z).

Note that
F_{B,j,\Gamma}
can be constructed from
E_{B,j,\Gamma}
by
(ßß)
.

Claim 5

F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
can be constructed from
E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof of Claim 5

For
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k_{B,j},r\}
if
k_{B,j}\geq 0
we set
\mathcal{W}_{\kappa}:=G_{B,j}|_{\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa}}
. Then we have for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa}

(since then
(\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa})_{t}=(K_{C_{B,j},\kappa})_{t}\cup(\mathcal{G}_{C_{B,j},\kappa})_{t}
)

\mathcal{W}_{\kappa}(t,z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Phi_{B,j}(t,z),&(t,z)\in K_{C_{B,j},\kappa},\\
\chi_{C_{B,j},\kappa}(t,z),&(t,z)\in\mathcal{G}_{C_{B,j},\kappa}.\end{array}\right.

We have that
\Phi_{B,j}|_{K_{C_{B,j},\kappa}}
can be constructed from
\mathcal{E}_{B,j}
by
(ß)
and that
\chi_{C_{B,j},\kappa}
can be constructed from
\mathcal{E}_{B,j}
by
(ßßß)
for
\kappa=r
or
\kappa\in\{k_{B,j},r\}
if
k_{B,j}\geq 0
. So with Remark 3.5 we see that
\mathcal{W}_{\kappa}
can be constructed from
\mathcal{E}_{B,j}
since every
g\in\mathcal{E}_{B,j}
is a positive definable function which depends only on
t
.

If
k_{B,j}<0
we have that
F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}=\mathcal{W}_{r}|_{Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}\setminus X}
. So
F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
can be constructed from
E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
and we are done. So suppose
k_{B,j}\geq 0
. Since for
\kappa\in\{k_{B,j},r\}

F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}|_{(\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa}\cap Z_{B,j})\setminus X}=\mathcal{W}_{\kappa}|_{(\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa}\cap Z_{B,j})\setminus X}

we have that
F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}|_{(\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa,}\cap Z_{B,j})\setminus X}
can be constructed from
E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}|_{(\mathcal{T}_{B,j,\kappa}\cap Z_{B,j})\setminus X}
. By Remark 3.5 we see that
F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
can be constructed from
E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
since every
g\in E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
is a positive definable function which depends only on
t
.
\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }5}

Finally we define
F:Z\to\mathbb{C}
in the following way.

If
(t,z)\in Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}\setminus X
for
B\in\mathcal{B}
and
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
let
F(t,z)=F_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}(t,z)
. If
(t,z)\in Z_{B,j,\Gamma}\setminus X
for
B\in\mathcal{B}
and
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
let
F(t,z)=F_{B,j,\Gamma}(t,z)
. If
(t,z)\in X
set
F(t,z)=f(t,z)
.

Then
F:Z\to\mathbb{C},(w,u,z)\mapsto F(w,u,z),
is a unary high parametric global complexification of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
since we have
F(w,u,z)=G(w,u,z)
for every
(w,u,z)\in Z
. Let

E:=\{g\mid g:Z\to\mathbb{C}\textnormal{ is a function with }g|_{X}\in E_{f},\textnormal{ }g|_{Z_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}\setminus X}\in E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}},\textnormal{ }

g|_{Z_{B,j,\Gamma}\setminus X}\in E_{B,j,\Gamma}\textnormal{ for }B\in\mathcal{B},j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}\}.

Here
E_{f}
is a set of positive definable functions on
X
such that every
g\in\log(E_{f})
is locally bounded in
(u,x)
and
f
can be constructed from
E_{f}
. (Since
f
is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,x)
such a set
E_{f}
exists.) By Remark 3.5 we see that
F
can be constructed from
E
(compare also with Definition 3.8).

So it remains to show that
E_{f}
,
E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
and
E_{B,j,\Gamma}
consists only of positive definable functions whose logarithms are locally bounded in
(u,z)
with respect to
Z
. (One sees then with Remark 3.13 that
E
is a set of locally bounded functions in
(u,z)
and therefore that
F
is a unary high parametric global complexification of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
which is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,z)
.)

Claim 6

Let
h\in E_{f}
. Then the function
\log(h)
is locally bounded in
(u,z)
with respect to
Z
.

Proof of Claim 6

Note that
h
is defined on
X
. Let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Let
(u_{0},z_{0})\in Z_{w}
. We show that
\log(h_{w})
is bounded at
(u_{0},z_{0})
and are done.

If
\textnormal{Im}(z_{0})\neq 0
we are clearly done by taking an open neighbourhood of
(u_{0},z_{0})
in
Z_{w}
disjoint from
X
. So assume
\textnormal{Im}(z_{0})=0
. Then
(u_{0},z_{0})\in X_{w}
and we find an open neighbourhood
U
of
(u_{0},z_{0})
in
X_{w}
such that
\log(h)|_{U}
is bounded. So take an open neighbourhood
V
of
(u_{0},z_{0})
in
Z_{w}
with
V\cap X\subset U
.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }6}

For
B\in\mathcal{B}
and
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
we set
\hat{E}_{B,j,\Gamma}:=\{\mathrm{exp}(\textnormal{Re}(g))\mid g\in\log(E_{B,j,\Gamma})\}
.

Claim 7

Let
B\in\mathcal{B}
and
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
. Let
h\in\hat{E}_{B,j,\Gamma}
. The function
\log(h)
is locally bounded in
(u,z)
with respect to
Z
.

Proof of Claim 7

Let
C:=C_{B,j}
,
\mathcal{K}:=Z_{B,j,\Gamma}\setminus X
,
w\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
and
(u_{0},z_{0})\in Z_{w}
. We show that
\log(h_{w})
is bounded at
(u_{0},z_{0})
.

If
(u_{0},z_{0})\notin\overline{\mathcal{K}_{w}}
we are clearly done by taking an open neighbourhood of
(u_{0},z_{0})
in
Z_{w}
disjoint from
\mathcal{K}_{w}
. So assume
(u_{0},z_{0})\in\overline{\mathcal{K}_{w}}
.

Case 1:

Let
h\in\mathcal{E}_{B,j}|_{\mathcal{K}}
. Suppose that
\log(h_{w})
is not bounded at
(u_{0},z_{0})
(i.e. at
u_{0}
). Then there is a definable curve
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to\mathcal{K}_{w}
such that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\gamma(y)=(u_{0},z_{0})
and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\log(h(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))}|=\infty
. Note that
(\gamma_{u},\textnormal{Re}(\gamma_{z}))
runs through
X_{B,j}
. By construction of
Z_{B,j}
(since
\zeta_{Z_{B,j}}
is bounded by
\mathrm{exp}(-|{\log(h)}|)
) we have
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{Im}(\gamma_{z}(y))=0
and thus
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. So we see

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{Z_{B,j}}(w,\gamma_{u}(y),\textnormal{Re}(\gamma_{z}(y)))=0,

a contradiction to the property (++).

Case 2:

Assume that
h\in E_{\Phi_{C}}|_{\mathcal{K}}
. Then
h=\mathrm{exp}(\Phi_{g})|_{\mathcal{K}}
for a
g\in\log(E_{C})
where
\Phi_{g}
is complex log-exp-analytically
(l,r)
-prepared in
z
for
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
l\in\{-1,...,e-1\}
. We show the statement by induction on
l
. If
l=-1
then
g=0
and the assertion follows.

l-1\to l
: Let

(r,\mathcal{Z},a,\mathrm{exp}(D_{0}),q,s,V,b,\mathrm{exp}(D),P):=

(r,\mathcal{Z}_{C,g}|_{\mathcal{K}},a_{C,g}|_{\mathcal{K}},\mathrm{exp}(D_{C,0,g})|_{\mathcal{K}},q_{C,g},s,V_{C,g},b_{C,g}|_{\mathcal{K}},\mathrm{exp}(D_{C,g})|_{\mathcal{K}},P_{C,g}).

Let
b:=(b_{1},...,b_{s})
,
\mathrm{exp}(D):=(\mathrm{exp}(D_{1}),...,\mathrm{exp}(D_{s}))
and
P:=(p_{1},...,p_{s})^{t}
. So we have

h(t,z)=a(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes q}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{0}(t,z))

V(b_{1}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{1}}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{1}(t,z)),...,b_{s}(t)(\sigma\mathcal{Z})^{\otimes p_{s}}(t,z)\mathrm{exp}(D_{s}(t,z)))

for
(t,z)\in\mathcal{K}
where
t:=(w,u)
. Let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{C}
be an open neighbourhood of
(u_{0},z_{0})
such that
(D_{0})_{w},...,(D_{s})_{w}
are bounded on
\hat{U}:=U\cap\mathcal{K}_{w}
. We may also assume that
a_{w}
and
(\Theta_{0})_{w},...,(\Theta_{r})_{w}
are bounded on
\hat{U}
. (Since we have that
\mathrm{exp}(a)\in\mathcal{E}_{C}
and
\mathrm{exp}(\Theta_{j})\in\mathcal{E}_{C}
for
j\in\{0,...,r\}
. Otherwise we derive the same contradiction as in Case 1.) Assume that there is an open neighbourhood
W\subset U
of
(u_{0},z_{0})
such that
h_{w}
is not bounded on
\hat{W}:=W\cap\mathcal{K}_{w}
. Then there is a definable curve
\gamma:\textnormal{}]0,1[\textnormal{}\to\hat{W}
such that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\mathcal{Z}^{\otimes q}(w,\gamma(y))}|=\infty
. Note that
\gamma
runs through
(\Gamma_{C})_{w}\setminus(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{w}
respectively
(\Gamma_{C})_{w}\setminus((\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{w}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,k_{C}})_{w})
if
k_{C}\geq 0
. Therefore
\gamma
runs also through
(H_{C,\textnormal{small}})_{w}
. By the proof of
(II_{l})
in Proposition 5.28 for
l\in\{0,...,r\}
(since
(\Theta_{0})_{w},...,(\Theta_{r})_{w}
are bounded on
\hat{W}_{w}
) and by construction of
\Gamma_{C}
we have that
\lim_{y\searrow 0}|{\gamma_{z}(y)-\mu_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}|=0
for
\kappa=r
or a
\kappa\in\{k_{C},r\}
if
k_{C}\geq 0
(since
\lim_{y\searrow 0}\mu_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\mathbb{R}
, compare also with Corollary 5.29(1)).

Fix such a
\kappa
. We see
(u_{0},z_{0})\in X_{w}
. Let
x_{0}:=z_{0}
. By definition of
(H_{C,\textnormal{small}})_{w}
we see that

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\mathfrak{d}_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{dist}(\mu_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),C_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))})=0.

So
(u_{0},x_{0})\in\overline{C_{w}}
. Fix
\epsilon>0
such that
Q^{m+1}((u_{0},x_{0}),\epsilon)\subset X_{w}
and
f_{w}(u,x)=p((u,x)-(u_{0},x_{0}))
for
(u,x)\in Q^{m+1}((u_{0},x_{0}),\epsilon)
where
p
is a convergent power series on
Q^{m+1}(0,\epsilon)
.

We have
\lim_{y\searrow 0}L_{C}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0
since otherwise we see that

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\zeta_{Z_{B,j}}(w,\gamma_{u}(y),\textnormal{Re}(\gamma_{z}(y)))=0

(since
(Z_{B,j})_{t}\subset\mathbb{R}+iQ(0,L_{C}(t))
for
t\in\pi(C)
). But this is a contradiction to property
(++)
.

So we see that
\gamma
is compatible with
C_{w}
. We have
\lim_{y\searrow 0}M_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0
and
\lim_{y\searrow 0}N_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=0
. (Since
M_{C,\kappa}
is
(C,\kappa)
-persistent and
N_{C,\kappa}
is
(C,\kappa)
-soft: Consider a suitable definable curve
\hat{\gamma}
in
C_{w}
converging to
(u_{0},x_{0})
with
\hat{\gamma}_{u}=\gamma_{u}
. Such a curve is compatible with
C_{w}
and exists by Remark 5.27(2) since

\lim_{y\searrow 0}\textnormal{dist}(\gamma_{x}(y),C_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))})=0.)

By passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary we may suppose the following.

(1)

We have
\gamma_{u}(y)\in Q^{m}(u_{0},\epsilon/2)
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
.

(2)

We have
|{\mu_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))-\gamma_{z}(y)}|<1/2\min\{M_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),\epsilon/2\}
and

C_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\cap B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),\epsilon)\neq\emptyset
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
.

(3)

We have either
N_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))>0
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
or
N_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=0
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
.

Assume the former in (3). The latter is handled completely similar. Then
\alpha_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))=2N_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))
for
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. Therefore

\alpha_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y))<1/2\min\{M_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),\epsilon/2\}

for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
after passing to a suitable subcurve of
\gamma
if necessary. We obtain with Claim 2 that
(w,\gamma_{u}(y))\in\hat{B}_{C,\kappa}
and therefore

B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),M_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))\setminus X_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\subset(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\setminus X_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}

respectively

B(\mu_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)),M_{C,\kappa}(w,\gamma_{u}(y)))\setminus X_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\subset(\mathcal{T}_{C,r}\cup\mathcal{T}_{C,k_{C}})_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}\setminus X_{(w,\gamma_{u}(y))}

if
k_{C}\geq 0
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
. This gives
(w,\gamma(y))\in\mathcal{T}_{C,r}\setminus X
respectively
(w,\gamma(y))\in(\mathcal{T}_{C,r}\cup\mathcal{T}_{C,k_{C}})\setminus X
for every
y\in\textnormal{}]0,1[
if
k_{C}\geq 0
with property (2). But this is a contradiction since
\gamma
runs through
(\Gamma_{C})_{w}\setminus(\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{w}
respectively through
(\Gamma_{C})_{w}\setminus((\mathcal{T}_{C,r})_{w}\cup(\mathcal{T}_{C,k_{C}})_{w})
if
k_{C}\geq 0
. So we obtain Claim 7.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim }7}

Similarly as in Claim 7 one sees that
\log(h)
is locally bounded in
(u,z)
with respect to
Z
if
h\in E_{B,j,\mathcal{T}}
for
B\in\mathcal{B}
and
j\in\{1,...,u_{B}\}
. This finishes the proof of Proposition 5.90.
\blacksquare

5.2.2
The Higher Dimensional Case

We show by induction on the number of variables that the class of restricted log-exp-analytic functions exhibits parametric global complexification. The ”highness” from the unary case above is necessary to enable the induction. We adapt Kaiser’s arguments from
[
17
]
to our situation and establish Theorem B.

Theorem B

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
w
range over
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
v
over
\mathbb{C}^{m}
. Let
X\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
be definable. Assume that
X_{t}
is open for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Let
f:X\to\mathbb{R},(t,w)\mapsto f(t,w),
be restricted log-exp-analytic in
w
such that
f_{t}
is real analytic for every
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
f
has an
m
-ary parametric global complexification
F:Z\to\mathbb{C},(t,v)\mapsto F(t,v),
which is restricted log-exp-analytic in
v
.

Proof

For
l\in\{1,...,m\}
let
u
range over
\mathbb{R}^{m-l}
and
x
over
\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Let
\pi_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{n},(t,u,x)\to t,
be the projection on the first
n
real coordinates. We show by induction on
l\in\{1,...,m\}
that
f:X\to\mathbb{R},(t,u,x)\mapsto f(t,u,x),
has an
l
-ary high parametric global complexification
F:Z\to\mathbb{C},(t,u,z)\mapsto F(t,u,z),
with respect to
(u,x)
where
F
is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,z)
.

l=1
: This follows from Proposition 5.90.

l-1\to l
: Given
x=(x_{1},...,x_{l})\in\mathbb{R}^{l}
we set
x^{\prime\prime}:=(x_{2},...,x_{l})
. The set

X_{(t,u,x_{1})}=\{x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{l-1}\mid(t,u,x_{1},x^{\prime\prime})\in X\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{l-1}

is open and the function
f_{(t,u,x_{1})}:X_{(t,u,x_{1})}\to\mathbb{R}
is real analytic since it coincides with
(f_{t})_{(u,x_{1})}
for
(t,u,x_{1})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-l}\times\mathbb{R}
. Let
\mathbb{R}^{n+m-l+1}
be the parameter space and write
\hat{X}
if we consider
X
as a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n+m-l+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{l-1}
. Moreover we write
\hat{f}
if we consider
f
as a function on
\hat{X}
. By the inductive hypothesis we find a definable set
\hat{Y}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+m-l+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{l-1}
such that
\hat{Y}_{t}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m-l+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{l-1}
is open for
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and an
(l-1)
-ary high parametric global complexification
\hat{G}:\hat{Y}\to\mathbb{C},(t,u,x_{1},z^{\prime\prime})\mapsto\hat{G}(t,u,x_{1},z^{\prime\prime}),
of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
which is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,x_{1},z^{\prime\prime})
. We set
G_{1}:=\textnormal{Re}(\hat{G}):\hat{Y}\to\mathbb{R}
and
G_{2}:=\textnormal{Im}(\hat{G}):\hat{Y}\to\mathbb{R}
. Note that
G_{1}|_{X}=f
and
G_{2}|_{X}=0
. Note that
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,x_{1},z^{\prime\prime})
. Let

Y:=\{(t,u,x_{1},z^{\prime\prime})\in\hat{Y}\mid(G_{j})_{t}\textnormal{ is real analytic at $(u,x_{1},z^{\prime\prime})$ for $j\in\{1,2\}$}\}.

Then
Y
is definable by a parametric version of Tamm’s theorem for restricted log-exp-analytic functions (see Corollary 3.27 in
[
28
]
). We have
X\subset Y\subset\hat{Y}
. Since real analytiticity is an open property we have that
Y_{t}
is open in
\mathbb{R}^{m-l+1}\times\mathbb{C}^{l-1}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Considering
z^{\prime\prime}
as a tuple of
2(l-1)
real variables we find with Proposition 5.90 a unary high parametric global complexification

F_{j}:Z_{j}\to\mathbb{C},(t,u,\hat{z},z^{\prime\prime})\mapsto F_{j}(t,u,\hat{z},z^{\prime\prime}),

for
G_{j}|_{Y}
with respect to
(u,x_{1},z^{\prime\prime})
which is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,\hat{z},z^{\prime\prime})
where
j\in\{1,2\}
. (Here
\hat{z}
is an additional variable which ranges over
\mathbb{C}
. Note also that
(Z_{j})_{t}
is open in
\mathbb{R}^{m-l}\times\mathbb{C}^{l}
for
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
X\subset Z_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
.)
(*)

Let

\tilde{Z}:=\{(t,u,z)\in Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}\mid(F_{1}+iF_{2})_{(t,u)}\textnormal{ is holomorphic at }z\}.

Then
\tilde{Z}
is definable and
F:=(F_{1}+iF_{2})|_{\tilde{Z}}:\tilde{Z}\to\mathbb{C}
is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,z)
with reference set
Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}
.

Let

\zeta_{\tilde{Z}}(t,u,x):=\sup\{r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\mid x+iQ^{l}(0,r)\subset\tilde{Z}_{(t,u)}\}.

Claim

Let
(t,u,x)\in X
. Then

\liminf_{(s,y)\to(u,x)}\zeta_{\tilde{Z}}(t,s,y)>0.

Proof of the claim

We find some
0<r
and an open neighbourhood
V
of
(u,x)
in
X_{t}
such that
f_{t}(s,y)=p((s,y)-(u,x))
for
(s,y)\in V
where
p
is a convergent power series on
Q^{m}(0,r)
. We find some
0<r^{\prime}<r/2
such that

U:=\textnormal{}Q^{m-l}(u,r^{\prime})\textnormal{}\times\textnormal{}]x_{1}-r^{\prime},x_{1}+r^{\prime}[\textnormal{}\times(Q^{l-1}(x^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime})+iQ^{l-1}(0,r^{\prime}))\subset\hat{Y}_{t}

and that
\tilde{G}_{t}(s,y_{1},z^{\prime\prime})=p((s,y_{1},z^{\prime\prime})-(u,x))
for all
(s,y_{1},z^{\prime\prime})\in U
. So for
(s,y)\in Q^{m}((u,x),r^{\prime})
we see that

y^{\prime\prime}+iQ^{l-1}(0,r^{\prime})\subset Y_{(t,s,y_{1})}.

So by
(*)
we find some
0<\epsilon<r^{\prime}/2
and an open neighbourhood
W
of
(u,x)
in
X_{t}
with
W\subset Q^{m}((u,x),r^{\prime})
such that for
(s,y)\in W
we have the following. We have
y+iQ^{l}(0,\epsilon)\subset(Z_{j})_{(t,s)}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
and
F_{(t,s)}(z)=p_{s-u}(z-x)
for all
z\in D^{l}(y,\epsilon)
. So by construction
y+iQ^{l}(0,\epsilon)\subset\tilde{Z}_{(t,s)}
for all
(s,y)\in W
. Hence

\liminf_{(s,y)\to(u,x)}\zeta_{\tilde{Z}}(t,s,y)\geq\epsilon>0.

\blacksquare_{\textnormal{Claim}}

Let

Z:=\{(t,u,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-l}\times\mathbb{C}^{l}\mid(t,u,\textnormal{Re}(z))\in X

\textnormal{ and }\textnormal{Im}(z)\in Q^{l}(0,\rho(t,u,\textnormal{Re}(z)))\}

where

\rho(t,u,x):=\liminf\limits_{(s,y)\to(u,x)}\zeta_{\tilde{Z}}(t,s,y)

for
(t,u,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-l}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
. Note that
Z_{t}
is open for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. (Compare with the proof of Claim 4 in Proposition 5.90: The function
\mathbb{R}^{m-l}\times\mathbb{R}^{l},(u,x)\mapsto\rho(t,u,x),
defines a lower semi-continuous function for every
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.) By the claim we have
X\subset Z
. So we see that
F|_{Z}
is an
l
-ary high parametric global complexification of
f
with respect to
(u,x)
. By the above we see that
F
is restricted log-exp-analytic in
(u,z)
. This finishes the proof of Theorem B.
{\blacksquare}

An immediate consequence of Theorem B is Theorem A.

Theorem A

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a definable open set. Let
f:U\to\mathbb{R}
be a real analytic restricted log-exp-analytic function. Then
f
has a global complexification which is again restricted log-exp-analytic, i.e. there is a definable open
V\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
with
U\subset V
and a holomorphic restricted log-exp-analytic
F:V\to\mathbb{C}
such that
F|_{U}=f
.

5.91 Corollary

(1)

Let
X\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}
be definable such that
X_{t}
is open for every
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and let
f:X\to\mathbb{R},(t,x)\mapsto f(t,x),
be log-analytic. Suppose that
f_{t}
is real analytic for every
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then there is a definable
V\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
with
U\subset V
such that
V_{t}
is open for every
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and a restricted log-exp-analytic
F:V\to\mathbb{C},(t,z)\mapsto F(t,z),
in
z
such that
F_{t}
is holomorphic for every
t\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
F|_{U}=f
.

(2)

Let
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be open. Let
g:U\to\mathbb{R}
be a real analytic log-analytic function. Then there is an open definable
V\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
with
U\subset V
and a restricted log-exp-analytic
G:V\to\mathbb{C}
such that
G|_{U}=g
.

Proof

(1): This follows directly from the fact that a log-analytic function is restricted log-exp-analytic in
x
.

(2): This follows directly from (1).
\blacksquare

To close this paper we want to discuss two open questions for further research in this context outgoing from Theorem A and Theorem B.

5.92 Question

Does a real analytic log-analytic function have a global complexification which is again log-analytic?

This question could be true simply by the observation that a log-analytic function extends piecewise to a holomorphic log-analytic function (by considering the single
\mathcal{L}_{\textnormal{an}}(^{-1},(\sqrt[n]{...})_{n=2,3,...},\log)
-terms). But to enable the induction on the number of variables one needs a preparation theorem for log-analytic functions with log-analytic data only. So one has to get better control on the exponentials which are involved in the construction of the coefficient, the center and the base functions of such a preparation. But such questions are pretty unsolved at present. An idea would be to adapt somehow the preparation theorem of Cluckers and Miller for constructible functions from
[
5
]
,
[
6
]
respectively
[
7
]
to the log-analytic case.

Since we established global complexification for real analytic restricted log-exp-analytic functions and the class of restricted log-exp-analytic functions is a proper subclass of the class of all definable functions (compare with Example 3.18) the following question arises.

5.94 Question

Does
\mathbb{R}_{\textnormal{an,exp}}
have global complexification?

With our results above this question can be reformulated in the following way: Is every real analytic definable function restricted log-exp-analytic? This may be true from the point of analysis: If the global exponential function comes not locally bounded into the game features like flatness may occur (compare with Example 3.18). But neither real analytic functions nor restricted log-exp-analytic functions are flat.
